# ChurchCore Development Plan

**Living Document** - Last Updated: October 8, 2026
**Version**: 2.2
**Purpose**: This is the single source of truth for all ChurchCore development. Every GitHub Issue, PR, sprint, and code review must reference this document. Update only via PR.

Visual companion: [docs/development-plan-visual.md](docs/development-plan-visual.md) summarizes the strategy, roadmap, boundary model, and Sprint 1 flow as diagrams. This document remains the source of truth.

## 0. MVP Roadmap to November 6, 2026 (tracker)

**This section is the single place for what's left before MVP, and it is the tracker.** Everything open is either a row in the tracker below or listed under "Deferred to after MVP". Nothing lives only in a Council write-up, the history further down, or chat. The owner set it on 2026-09-26.

### 0.1 Target and rules

- **MVP finish date: Friday, November 6, 2026** (moved from October 30 by the owner on 2026-09-29, with about 5.5 factory days cut; see §0.4).
- **MVP means:**
  - all five competitive gaps meet their definition of done (in §0.3);
  - the production-safety track (the `S` rows) is complete;
  - the release checklist (§0.6) passes.
- **The five competitive gaps**, ranked against Planning Center Online, Breeze ChMS and Tithe.ly (Council Review 9, re-verified in code 2026-09-25):
  1. Service planning depth: setlists, song library, volunteer-role matching and rotation.
  2. A phone-first member experience and a check-in kiosk.
  3. Recurring giving and donor statements.
  4. Migration from incumbents, with reconciliation.
  5. Provider breadth: Resend (accounting export moved after MVP on 2026-09-29).
- **Retired gates.** The former Phase A–D gates are retired as MVP criteria. So is their blocker ("a church onboards uncoached" plus "a real-export migration dry-run"). Migration is validated against fixtures built from the incumbents' documented formats (neither Planning Center nor Breeze publishes its export header row, so the fixtures are labelled by confidence; G4.1, 2026-10-07), not a partner church's data. Real export files from a pilot church are owner action O15.
- **MVP readiness 92/100 since G4.2 merged and Gap 4 closed; 93 is proposed on G2.1's merge (Council Review 47, 2026-10-08, diff-scoped, five distinct agents under Council v2; synthesis status AMENDED, R1-R7 landed in `e07e73f`; a recommendation, not a final score).** **G4.2 merged as #195 (`28f1bda`) on 2026-10-08: Gap 4 is CLOSED 2026-10-08** (4 of 5 gaps closed; Gap 2 needs G2.2). **Owner action O16 is done:** the owner authorized it and the orchestrator ran `supabase db push --linked` for `20261009000000` before the merge on 2026-10-08, then confirmed by read-only query the 4 outcome columns, the immutability trigger, the `SECURITY INVOKER` function with no anon execute, and the update policy. **G2.1 (phone-first member flows) is built and Council Review 47-clean, done pending merge** on `feat/phone-first-member-g2-1` (`abffd6e` feature, `e07e73f` Council fixes; frontend only: no migration, RLS, server-action or API change; no PR yet). **What shipped:** at 390x844 (and 360 for overflow) member home, schedule, giving and family have every control at least 44 px (a member-scoped `.touch-44` in `app/globals.css`, using descendant selectors because Mantine 9 sets `--button-height-*` on the button element, so the brief's inheritance assumption was wrong), no sideways scroll, one column, and the primary action on the first screen (`data-primary-action`); home and giving dates use the church timezone (`formatInstantParts` in `lib/church-time.ts`), so an 8 pm Sunday event no longer reads Monday; `/app/calendar` has a real h1 in the hub (not the shell); service-plan stat tiles stack on a phone; `RoleTypeManager` buttons name their role; number-input spinners are hidden on member pages. These close the four defects named in Council Reviews 14 and 15. **Owner decisions (2026-10-08):** keep the 2-up quick actions; first screen with no sticky bars; "Give now" is the giving primary action; the giving Type column drops below `sm`; reorder only if a test shows it does not fit. **Council fixes (R1-R7):** dialogs render in a portal outside `.touch-44` (family edit, profile edit, recurring-gift drawer) now carry the class; the e2e opens them and runs the 44 px collector inside; member home banner/content in a `Stack`; `MemberFamilyEdit` `triggerProps` typed and spread before `onClick`; a synchronous `useRef` latch on confirm and decline; e2e shift moved to +60 days with a scoped Confirm locator; e2e DB helpers refuse a non-loopback host unless `CI=true`. The mobile-layout helper has negative controls proving it can fail (`.touch-44` off flags 30 and 36 px on home; family modal class off flags a 28 px close button and a 36 px input). **New tracker rows:** S28 (home "upcoming serving" reads `event_rosters` while the schedule reads `volunteer_shifts`; plus the shift-answer status-transition rule) and S29 (demo seed has no future shift for the demo member). **Recounted from the tracker:** 33 Must rows, 27 merged (G4.2 as #195); remaining Must work, counting G2.1 until it merges (the standing convention): 6 rows (G2.1, G2.2, T1b, T2, R1, R2), 8.5 factory days (1.5 + 1.5 + 1.0 + 1.5 + 2.5 + 0.5), against 22 working days (Oct 8 - Nov 6: 2 + 4 x 5): 13.5 days of slack; **after G2.1 merges: 5 rows (G2.2, T1b, T2, R1, R2), 7.0 days, 15.0 days of slack.** Next: open the G2.1 PR, get CI green, read GitHub's review; then G2.2 (kiosk, M4 due Oct 30). **Recorded, not fixed:** S28 and S29; English-only new labels (the Calendar heading, the mobile Recurring badge, the `RoleTypeManager` labels), covered by the localization coverage gap; the local-SQL branch of `respondToShiftAction` lacks the Supabase branch's future-date guard and row-count check (deprecated path). **Agent claims wrong or unsupported (3 seat claims, 2 researcher errors):** Agent 4 said O13 is open (done) and listed "T3" as remaining Must work for the second round running (folded into R1 on 2026-09-29), and scored a "Teacher" role that does not exist; the codebase researcher said Mantine's `sm` triggers at about 390 px (it is 48em, 768 px) and proposed a thumb-reach test that measures the top of the screen. **Verified (orchestrator-run, on `e07e73f`):** `npx vitest run` 222 files / 2,909 tests pass; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run lint` 0 errors (1 warning); `npm run test:surfaces` OK. **Builder-run:** `npm run build` OK; `member-phone-first.spec.ts` plus `member-mobile-foundation.spec.ts` 27/27 twice with retries 0; negative controls as above; page-role sweep `calendar|member` 170/170 on rerun after a local sign-in redirect flake. **Not verified: CI (`verify`, 4 `e2e` shards) and GitHub's PR review have not run because no PR exists; a brand-new member with extra alerts above the quick actions on home (first-screen check passed only for the seeded member with a complete profile).** Details: `docs/factory-runs/2026-10-08-g2-1-phone-first-member.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-08-council-review-47-synthesis.md`.

a church-admin update policy limited by column grant to those four columns; a `SECURITY INVOKER` bulk function (`record_import_row_outcomes`, chunks of 500); an immutability trigger (outcomes settable only while the batch is `committing`, never changed once set, every role); the batch summary now merged, not replaced; the report page `/app/church-admin/imports/[batchId]` (counts per entity, giving totals source / written at commit / current / difference, the general-ledger note, mismatches, a separate "changed since import" list, skipped and rejected rows with reasons) and the audited CSV route `GET /api/church-admin/imports/[batchId]/report`; "Recent imports" (last 20 per type) on all five import pages. **Owner decisions (2026-10-07):** "changed since import" is separate and does not count against the import; the CSV is minimal (no names, emails or phones); batches keep their outcomes as long as the batch exists and the last 20 are listed; four brief defaults (giving updates compared on amount, date and fund; already-a-member counts as written; rows never attempted are mismatches; difference is source minus written at commit). **Bugs found while building, already on `main`:** the Supabase commit replaced `import_batches.summary`, losing the dry-run counts and ignored columns (the codebase researcher said it was already merged; it had read the local-SQL branch, not the Supabase one); existing-record lookups in giving, attendance, groups and events ignored errors; an importer's own page could crash on a failed secondary list. **Council fixes (R1-R10):** the immutability trigger (a church admin could have PATCHed outcomes to hide a mismatch), a refused final flush marks outcomes "not fully recorded", the RPC row count is checked, commits revalidate the page and Recent imports leaves out dry runs, back link by importer, a failed Recent imports read is contained, status labels, `finishImportBatch` guarded on `committing`, a fund added after import shows as changed, numeric CSV cells stay numbers. **5 agent claims wrong or unsupported** (see the synthesis). New [ADR 0029](docs/adr/0029-import-row-outcomes-column-grant-invoker-function-trigger.md). **New tracker rows:** owner action **O16** (apply migration `20261009000000` to the hosted database **before** merging this PR: a commit running the new code against the old schema fails at its first outcome flush); S27 (release or recover a batch stuck in `committing`, and move `summary` writes behind a function so an admin cannot edit `mismatchCount`). **Recounted from the tracker:** 33 Must rows, 26 merged (G4.1 as #194); remaining Must work, counting G4.2 until it merges (the standing convention): 7 rows (G4.2, G2.1, G2.2, T1b, T2, R1, R2), 10.0 factory days (1.5 + 1.5 + 1.5 + 1.0 + 1.5 + 2.5 + 0.5, the Est. column of each row), against 22 working days (Oct 8 - Nov 6: 2 + 4 x 5), 12 days of slack; **after G4.2 merges: 6 rows (G2.1, G2.2, T1b, T2, R1, R2), 8.5 days, 13.5 days of slack.** Next: open the G4.2 PR once O16 is done, then M4's remaining rows (G2.1, G2.2, due Oct 30). **Recorded, not fixed:** S27 above; the insert-order assumption in `insertProfileChunk` (Review 45 D4); only the CSV download is audited, not viewing the report; whether a giving `sourceId` in the CSV can carry a donor identifier is UNVERIFIED; the report re-reads up to 5,000 records per page view (about 0.75 s locally); imported gifts still do not post to the general ledger (the report says so). **Verified (orchestrator-run, on `dcc6c14`):** `npx vitest run` 221 files / 2,895 tests pass; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run lint` 0 errors (1 warning); `npm run test:surfaces` OK; `npm run build` OK. - [Superseded 2026-10-06: G5.1 merged as #188 (`12220b1`), O12 is done and Gap 5 is closed; "pending merge, no PR yet" and "Gap 5 does NOT close on merge" below were true when written.] **Prior: MVP readiness 87/100 (Council Review 43, 2026-10-05, diff-scoped, five distinct agents under Council v2; synthesis status AMENDED, all three required fixes landed in `a961d21`), up from 86; it reaches 88 when owner action O12 confirms a live Resend send and Gap 5 closes.** G5.1 (Resend live, F2, ADR 0006) is built and Council Review 43-clean, **done pending merge** on `feat/resend-live-g5-1` (`93e883c` build, `a961d21` fixes; no PR yet, CI not run). One `selectEmailProvider()` routes both email paths (the communications queue and the direct `sendEmail()` used by receipts and notices) to Resend when `RESEND_API_KEY` and `RESEND_FROM_EMAIL` are set, else SendGrid, else not configured (production without keys still returns `provider_not_configured`). Resend requests follow its documented contract with an `Idempotency-Key`; both providers' HTTP statuses map to one shared set of codes (one shared transient constant); the provider is recorded on `communication_logs.provider`. A one-off `scripts/resend-unsent-receipts.mjs` (dry run by default, `--apply` to send, counts only) re-sends unsent donation receipts through the same claim/lease code (owner decision). No migration. **Found by the orchestrator from Resend's idempotency docs, all five seats missed it:** the first draft used a random key on the first send and `comm-log:<id>:attempt:<n>` on each retry, so a send that reached Resend but timed out on our side would be sent again under a new key. Fixed: the log row's id is created before the first send and `comm-log:<id>` is the key for the first send and every retry; other keys are prefixed by kind (`donation:`, `registration-payment:`); giving statements use `comm-log:<claimRowId>` (a fresh key per claim, an orchestrator change after the builder). Also fixed: `send-email.ts` is now `server-only`; the history page shows a plain-language failure reason and offers Retry only when the server-side `shouldRetryDelivery` says it can work. **Gap 5 does NOT close on merge.** The M3 line reads "Resend live", meaning email actually leaves production, so Gap 5 closes only after O12 confirms one real send (Agent 4 said it closes on merge; wrong). **New owner action O12** (see the tracker). **7 agent claims wrong or unsupported** (see the synthesis). **Recorded, not fixed:** no in-app provider-status indicator (S12 stays open); the Resend error-type field (`name` or `type`) is unverified (only the 409 `invalid_idempotent_request` distinction uses it); no live Resend call has been made; Resend's caching of failed responses for a reused key is undocumented (mitigated by per-claim statement keys). **Recounted from the tracker:** 33 Must rows, 22 merged (G3.3b merged as #184); remaining Must work, counting G5.1 until it merges (the standing convention): 11 rows (S11, G5.1, T1a, G2.1, G2.2, G4.1, G4.2, T1b, T2, R1, R2), 14.0 factory days, against 25 working days (Oct 5 - Nov 6), 11 days of slack. Next: S11, T1a for M3 (Fri Oct 23), plus O12. **Verified (orchestrator-run, after `a961d21`):** `npx vitest run` 206 files / 2,544 tests pass; `npm run lint` 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run test:surfaces` OK; builder: `npm run build` compiled, e2e `api-cron` + `api-webhooks` + `church-admin-readiness` 45 passed, the re-send script's dry run against local Supabase counted 2 and sent nothing. **Not verified: CI (`verify`, 4 `e2e` shards) has not run because no PR exists; GitHub's PR review has not run; no live Resend call has been made.** Details: `docs/factory-runs/2026-10-05-g5-1-resend-live.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-05-council-review-43-synthesis.md`.

Gap 3 closes on merge** against its own definition of done: G3.1-G3.3 and G3.3b are done, and the giving journeys in CI now include recurring "change" (the orchestrator found, while checking an agent's claim, that `tests/e2e/recurring-giving.spec.ts` never changed a gift; the step now exists). When a registration payment succeeds, the Stripe webhook sends one receipt (church, event and its church-local time, amount, "not a tax-deductible donation"), claimed with a 5-minute lease on `event_registration_payments` (migration `20261007000000`, additive, nullable `receipt_claimed_at`/`receipt_sent_at`) and marked sent only after the provider accepts; the previously unchecked payment update now checks its error. **Found by Agent 5 (Security), a pre-existing bug fixed this round:** `sendEmail()` returned `{ accepted: true }` in every environment when `SENDGRID_API_KEY`/`SENDGRID_FROM_EMAIL` were unset, so a keyless production deploy recorded donation receipts and failure notices as sent that never went out (the class Council Reviews 22 and 23 fixed in the communications adapters; this direct path was missed). The three callers (donation receipts, recurring failure notices, registration receipts) treat that as non-retryable: release the claim, log a warning, leave the sent marker unset (the receipt stays visibly unsent) and answer the webhook 200, so Stripe is not retried for days; every other refusal still throws and is retried. **Until an email provider is configured, receipts stay unsent by design: G5.1 must make receipts actually send in production.** **6 agent claims wrong or unsupported** (see the synthesis). **Recorded, not fixed:** a possible second (Stripe) receipt if a church enables Stripe receipts (same as donations), English-only receipts (S13), and unsent receipts not yet visible to operators (S12). **Owner actions:** O11 (apply migration `20261007000000` after merge); O8 and O10 done 2026-10-05; O9 key set, live `/hq` call still owed. **Recounted from the tracker:** 33 Must rows, 21 merged (G3.3b makes 22 on merge); remaining Must work, counting G3.3b until it merges (the standing convention): 12 rows (G3.3b, S11, G5.1, T1a, G2.1, G2.2, G4.1, G4.2, T1b, T2, R1, R2), 14.5 factory days, against 25 working days (Oct 5 - Nov 6), 10.5 days of slack. Next: S11, G5.1, T1a for M3 (Fri Oct 23). **Verified (orchestrator-run, after `149af55`):** `npx vitest run` 202 files / 2,459 tests pass; `npm run lint` 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run test:surfaces` OK; builder: `npm run build` succeeds, `npm run lint:migrations` PASS, migration applies to a fresh reset, `recurring-giving.spec.ts` + `api-webhooks.spec.ts` + `giving-statements.spec.ts` 31/31 pass locally (including the new "change" step), column-references DB test passes. **Not verified: CI (`verify`, 4 `e2e` shards) has not run because no PR exists; no real SendGrid or Stripe call has been made.** Details: `docs/factory-runs/2026-10-05-g3-3b-registration-receipt.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-05-council-review-42-synthesis.md`.

- [Superseded: G3.3 merged as #181 (`b29a255`) on 2026-10-04 and G3.3b is now built (Council Review 42); "pending merge, no PR yet" and "Gap 3 is NOT closed until G3.3b lands" below were true when written.] **Prior: MVP readiness 84/100** (Council Review 40, 2026-10-04, diff-scoped, five distinct agents under Council v2; synthesis status AMENDED, all required fixes landed in `55a2418`), up from 82: **G3.3 (year-end giving statements) is built and Council Review 40-clean, done pending merge on `feat/giving-statements-g3-3` (commits `60d912b` build, `55a2418` Council fixes). No PR exists yet and CI has not run.** An admin previews per-donor statements for a church-local date range (default: last calendar year), seeing who will be emailed and why anyone is skipped; downloads named-gift PDFs (`pdf-lib`, standard fonts); and sends an idempotent batch email whose body is the statement (claim-before-send under a partial unique index, migration `20261005000000`). A member downloads their own full statement, including their own anonymous gifts, from `/app/member/giving`. **Owner decision 2026-10-04 (after the factory validator's two High findings): anonymity holds against staff end to end.** Staff see named gifts only; anonymous gifts appear as one unattributed aggregate line; the admin PDF is named gifts only; the donor gets the full statement; statement send records carry no recipient. **Found by the orchestrator, not any Council seat (fixed):** the first build wrote `recipient_id` on statement send records, and the Communications history renders that by name to pastors and secretaries, exposing every anonymous-only donor and every named donor to roles with no giving access; now `recipient_id: null`. Also fixed: CR/LF stripped from the email subject (defence in depth), a 10-year range cap, a warning when a stale `sending` claim is released, and the rollback line in the migration. **7 agent claims wrong or unsupported against source**, including the Security seat's High "email header injection" (both providers take the subject as a JSON field, not an SMTP header). **Gap 3 is NOT closed until G3.3b lands** (new Must row, ~0.5 day, M3: ChurchCore's own receipt for a paid event registration, split out of G3.3 by owner decision 2026-10-04). M3 also needs S11, G5.1 and T1a. Owner action O8: apply migration `20261005000000` to the hosted database after merge. **Recounted from the tracker (2026-10-04):** 32 Must rows before this round (20 done; 12 open; the 4 deferred rows are not counted in the 32), now 33 with G3.3b. Remaining Must work, counting G3.3 until it merges (the standing convention): 13 rows (G3.3, G3.3b, S11, G5.1, T1a, G2.1, G2.2, G4.1, G4.2, T1b, T2, R1, R2), 16.5 factory days, against 25 working days (Oct 5 – Nov 6), about 8.5 days of slack (10.5 once G3.3 itself merges); slack is up from about 5.5 because G3.1 and G3.2 merged and stop counting, not because capacity grew. **Verified (orchestrator-run, after `55a2418`):** `npx vitest run` 199 files / 2,403 tests pass; `npm run lint` 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning in `localization-governance.config.mjs`); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run test:surfaces` OK; `npm run lint:migrations` PASS; migration `20261005000000` applies to a freshly reset local database (`npm run setup:e2e -- --reset`); `npm run test:e2e:local` on `giving-statements.spec.ts` and `api-session-routes.spec.ts`: 49 passed. **Not yet verified: CI (`verify`, 4 `e2e` shards) has not run, because no PR exists.** Details: `docs/factory-runs/2026-10-04-g3-3-giving-statements.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-04-council-review-40-synthesis.md`.

- **Prior: MVP readiness 82/100** (Council Review 38, 2026-10-02, diff-scoped, four distinct agents), up from 80: **G3.1 (recurring gifts) and G3.2 (installments from Stripe webhooks, retry-safe webhook) merged to `main` as PR #177 (`5253915`) on 2026-10-03, shipped together on one branch (owner decision) so no card is ever charged without being recorded.** `recurring_gifts` (migration `20261004000000`) holds a member's plan (amount, fund, weekly/biweekly/monthly, start date), backed by a Stripe subscription on the church's own connected account (ADR 0025) — members read their own, managers their church's, only the server writes. Starting today charges the first installment in the card step; a future start (up to a year ahead) saves the card via a SetupIntent and trials to a charge at the church's own midnight on its start date (the card step gains a setup mode). Members set up, change (no proration), pause, resume and cancel from `/app/member/giving`; church admins see a Recurring tab, never naming an anonymous giver, and can pause, resume or cancel, audited. The old `cancelRecurringDonationAction`, which worked on `donations` rows nothing had ever created, is removed. `invoice.paid` records each installment as a donation keyed by its invoice (so a Stripe retry can't double-record it) and completes it through a shared `completeDonation` — used by the webhook, the member confirm and the public stub path alike — succeeded, then the ledger post (repeatable; a half-written journal is replaced), then the receipt (claimed before sending, released if refused), then `completed_at`, written last. `invoice.payment_failed` records the failure, tells the donor once, and marks the gift past due; `customer.subscription.updated`/`deleted` sync the gift, ordered by event time, and a cancelled gift never comes back. The webhook route now answers 5xx on a handler failure, so Stripe retries, instead of 200 — G3.2's widened definition of done (Council Review 31). `/give/[slug]` now takes real one-time gifts on the church's own account in production — rate-limited, fund-validated, cancellable while unpaid, recorded by the webhook, Frequency removed — closing G3.1's widened scope (Council Review 37): it had been a mock-up reachable only in demo mode, showing "thank you" after a 600ms wait without charging or recording anything. **Found by the orchestrator (fixed):** the mock public giving page, above; `cancelStripeSubscription` called `POST /v1/subscriptions/:id/cancel`, an endpoint that doesn't exist for subscriptions (Stripe cancels one with `DELETE /v1/subscriptions/:id`), so no recurring gift could ever have been cancelled at Stripe — pre-existing, the same class as Council Review 34's malformed PaymentIntent parameter, fixed (`8149031`) with a test pinning the request; the webhook answering 200 on handler errors, above. **Fixed after Council (`2d2a522`):** a subscription created before a later failure is now cancelled at Stripe too, so no orphan is left; a gift whose card was never confirmed stays incomplete (no "active" sync, no failure email) instead of acting like a real gift; a retry e2e proving a signed `invoice.paid` resumes an installment an earlier attempt left half-finished with no second receipt, and a repeated delivery records nothing twice; the admin "Recurring" tab label is in i18n (en/es/es-PR); the start date states its time zone; focus moves to the card step when it replaces the form; the receipt copy now says a charged gift's receipt "will follow shortly" rather than overstating it's already on its way. **6 agent claims wrong or unsupported against source:** a "Critical: missing role check" on the member recurring actions (they're scoped to the caller's own profile, like the existing one-time gift actions); "the manifest should mark actions public" (no such field exists); "teal violates the design system" (ADR 0026 keeps teal as the secondary accent); "demo card numbers above the real Stripe form" (no Stripe form renders in demo mode); "no warning for a member with no email" (starting a gift requires one); and Agent 4 inverting the actual flow — "the SetupIntent is confirmed before the subscription exists" when it's the reverse — see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory. **Owner decisions (2026-10-02):** a member updating the card on a recurring gift, through Stripe's SetupIntent and the existing card step's setup mode, is a new Should row, **S20** (~1 day); the existing cron cancelling an incomplete gift's subscription after 24 hours (an abandoned setup with no card, open at Stripe until its start date) is a new Should row, **S21** (~0.5 day); an admin creating a recurring gift on someone's behalf is a new post-MVP row (§0.5); **O7 widens** — the platform's Connect webhook needs three more events (`invoice.paid`, `invoice.payment_failed`, `customer.subscription.updated`; `docs/setup/production-deployment.md` is already updated), and the real test-mode run must also cover a future-dated recurring gift (Stripe returning `pending_setup_intent`, unverified until then) and a paid installment. **This close-out also found and corrected two stale statuses: D1 and G3.0c had each already merged (PR #176, PR #175) before this round started, but their rows still read "pending merge."** Correcting that, together with fixing G3.2's Est. column (2 → 2.5 — the +0.5 Council Review 31 widening had only ever been stated in prose, never applied to the table, understating every prior total by half a day) and counting G3.1/G3.2 themselves as Done-pending-PR (still counted, per the standing convention, until a PR exists): **remaining Must work is now 14 rows, 20.5 factory days, against 26 working days (Oct 2 – Nov 6), about 5.5 days of slack** — up from about 4, mostly from the D1/G3.0c status correction rather than new capacity, since G3.1 and G3.2 themselves still count until merged. **Verified (re-run by the Documenter):** `tsc` clean; `npm run lint` exit 0; `npm run test:surfaces` OK; `npx vitest run` (**2,209 tests / 185 files**); `npm run test:db` (**91 tests**, including the new `tests/database/recurring-gifts-rls.test.ts`); `npm run lint:migrations` PASS; `npm run build` compiled. Full local e2e: 816 passed, 0 failed (11 known local "denied" timeouts, all passing on retry). `tests/e2e/recurring-giving.spec.ts` with `api-webhooks`: 25/25 — setup/pause/resume/cancel, the admin list masking an anonymous giver, a public one-time gift recorded/completed/receipted, and the retry e2e. Commit signatures verified. One new migration, `20261004000000_recurring_gifts.sql`. **Update (Documenter, Council Review 39 close-out, 2026-10-03):** merged to `main` as PR #177 (`5253915`), CI green including all four `e2e` shards and `verify` (confirmed via `gh pr checks 177`); the migration was applied to the hosted database the same day (dry run, then push — `gh`/`supabase db push` reported "Remote database is up to date" after). Volunteer Scheduling unchanged at ~90%. Not more, because G3.3 (statements) is open and nothing has run against real Stripe yet (O7).

- **Prior: MVP readiness 80/100 at Review 37** (2026-10-02, diff-scoped, four distinct agents), holding at 80 (not up): **D1 (ChurchCore design system through the theme, ADR 0026) is done, pending merge, as a new Must row.** The owner adopted the ChurchCore Design System & UI/UX Specification (written for sister apps ChurchCore LMS and Orthos) and chose visual parity through the Mantine theme, not a rewrite — Mantine, ChurchCore's own i18n and `church_id` all stay. The app is now dark-first: `slate-950` canvas, `slate-900` surfaces, indigo primary with a soft glow, Inter type, `rounded-2xl` containers and `rounded-xl` controls, rendered dark on the server so there's no light flash. About 390 hard-coded light-theme colour literals across 58 files were remapped to the new palette, by property and luminance; `churchBlue` (~100 uses) is kept as an alias of indigo so those components follow the new primary with no edits. **Council Review 37** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-02-council-review-37-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, four distinct agents) confirmed D1 meets ADR 0026 and all seven items on the spec's section 8 checklist, then found and fixed, owner-approved, in `e1dbbf6`: calendar month cells that were white behind white day numbers; the check-in badge preview's dark ink on its (intentionally) white printed-badge card; Mantine grey CSS variables the literal-based sweep couldn't see (the public giving canvas, the sign-in icon circle, the custom-reports panel and pills, an `/hq` divider); off-palette control-plane colours; input/checkbox/radio outlines moved to slate-500 (3.9:1, meeting WCAG 1.4.11, was 1.8:1); and four calendar category colours changed so each chip (≥4.5:1 text) and dot (≥3:1) both pass. **A new ratchet test, `components/theme-provider.test.ts`, now enforces ADR 0026 decision 4:** colour literals in `app/` and `components/` may only go down from this round's baseline (**374** remaining — real colours and dark-token values that are expected to stay literal, not an error), and the pre-ADR-0026 light theme's palette is banned outright. **4 agent claims wrong or unsupported against source:** a category-colour contrast figure given as "~1.2:1" (actual 1.72–2.66:1; the finding was real, the number wasn't); a yellow alert's contrast given as "~2:1" with white text (actual about 8.5:1 on the rendered `#733c00`, sampled from the screenshot); "ADR 0026 names Portuguese as Must" (it doesn't — it only gives `pt` its own row); and the white badge preview's background framed as the defect itself (it's intentional; the text colour was the defect) — see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory. **Found while reviewing, pre-existing, outside D1's diff: `/give/[slug]` works only in demo mode** — outside it the route returns `notFound`, because the production query of `public_giving_pages` was never written. **Folded into G3.1's scope**, below. **Owner decisions (2026-10-02):** Portuguese (`pt`, full key parity plus ADR 0009 governance and a native-speaker review) is a new **Should** row (~2 days), after MVP Must work; synthetic QA tenants (`churches.is_synthetic`, blocking live Stripe/email/SMS/webhooks) is a new **Should** row (~1 day); a light-mode option is a new **post-MVP** row (~1.5 days, needs the swept literals turned into CSS variables first); D2 (visual parity beyond colour — page scaffold, stat cards, table headers) is a new **post-MVP** row (~3–4 days). **Readiness holds at 80** — D1 is the visual foundation; it closes none of the five competitive gaps, and mobile (Gap 2) was re-checked and isn't worse. **D1 is a new Must row, built and shipped the same day it was proposed (no PR yet, so it still counts as outstanding): remaining Must work is now 16 rows, 22 factory days, against 26 working days (Oct 2 – Nov 6), about 4 days of slack** (down from about 5) — D1 consumed about 1 day of that slack. **Verified (re-run by the Documenter):** `tsc` clean; `npm run lint` exit 0; `npm run test:surfaces` OK; `npx vitest run` (**2,125 tests / 180 files**, including the new `theme-provider.test.ts` ratchet); `npm run build` compiled. Full local e2e: 813 passed; the only failures were known local "denied" timeouts, all 64 passing when re-run serially; one real overflow (the member directory with a long email) was found and fixed, after which the mobile suite passed 12/12. Targeted e2e after the Council fixes: 39/39. Visual check: before/after screenshots of 25+ pages, every role, desktop and phone. Commit signatures verified. No migrations. **No PR exists yet for this branch, so the CI `e2e` job has not run** — not independently confirmed; a draft PR is needed before this is treated as merge-ready. Volunteer Scheduling unchanged at ~90%. Not more, because D1 closes no competitive gap and G3.1, G3.2, G3.3 and O7 are all still open.

- **Prior: 80/100 at Review 36** (2026-10-02, diff-scoped, four distinct agents), up from 79: **G3.0c (card form for paid event registrations) is built and Council Review 36-clean, pending PR.** A paid registration from the public portal or a member now gets Stripe's card form on the church's own connected account (ADR 0025), replacing the old "Secure payment ready" panel, which had no way to pay; refuses before anything is written when the church can't take payments; Stripe errors are no longer swallowed; leaving the payment step cancels the PaymentIntent then the registration, freeing its place. One shared server-only helper (`lib/event-registration-payment.ts`) serves the public and member paths; the giving card step is reused, with Back optional. **The admin "add registrant" path doesn't refuse (owner-confirmed 2026-10-02):** before this branch it created a PaymentIntent and discarded the client secret, so nobody could ever pay it; it now creates no PaymentIntent, records the payment as due, and tells the admin to collect it in person — an admin can always add a walk-in, even at an unconnected church, superseding the row's literal refuse-everywhere wording for the admin path only. **Found by the orchestrator while building, fixed:** `registerForEventAction`, a `"use server"` export, had no session or role check — RLS (`can_manage_church`) was its only gate; it now has the event page's own gate (church admin, pastor, in the session's church). **Two of the five call sites the row originally listed are dead local-SQL branches** (`shouldUseLocalTenantFallback()` always returns `false`), left untouched under the Supabase-only mandate. **Council Review 36** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-02-council-review-36-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, four distinct agents) confirmed G3.0c meets its row, then fixed, owner-approved, in `50dc819`: the payment step now says up front that closing the window, or choosing Cancel registration, cancels the registration without charge; a registration's card step labels its cancel "Cancel registration" (new optional `cancelLabel`, giving keeps "Cancel"); the payment step has a heading, which takes focus when it replaces the form; a PaymentIntent left open after the payment row fails is now cancelled at Stripe instead of orphaned. **6 agent claims wrong or unsupported against source** — a "Critical tenant violation" in the public cancel action (its proof is the registration/PaymentIntent id pair, and writes are already scoped to the matched row's church); "the secretary role isn't tested as denied" (it is); "the `size=\"lg\"` modal isn't responsive" (Mantine's `max-width: 100%` shrinks it); "the old admin path never created a PaymentIntent" (it did, and discarded the client secret); **Prior: 79/100 at Review 35** (2026-10-02, diff-scoped, four distinct agents), up from 77: **G3.0 (card form for online giving) merged to `main` as PR #172 (`13d0413`) since the last entry**, and **S16** (patch `next` to 16.3.8 for the critical `next/og` RCE advisory) merged as PR #173 the same round — neither changes the Must-row count or total (G3.0 drops out of "remaining"; S16 is Should). **G3.0b (Stripe Connect: each church's payments now run on its own Stripe account) is built and Council Review 35-clean, pending merge.** `church_payment_accounts` (migration `20261003000000`) holds one Stripe account per church — church-admin read-only RLS, no anon access, written only by the server; `donations` and `event_registration_payments` each gain a `stripe_account_id` column so confirm, cancel, refunds and subscription cancels reach the right account later. `/api/stripe/connect/start` and `/callback` are church-admin only, with an HMAC-signed, 15-minute OAuth state bound to the church and admin who started it. Every live Stripe call carries `Stripe-Account`; Stripe.js loads on the church's own account; a live call made without one throws instead of charging the platform. The webhook accepts the Connect endpoint's secret, scopes a connected event to the account's church, and handles `account.updated`/`account.application.deauthorized`. **Found while building, fixed before Council review:** `stripeRequest` misread Connect OAuth's flat error shape, so every OAuth failure read as "Stripe 401" — disconnecting an account the church had already revoked at Stripe could never succeed; fixed, along with narrowing the tolerated-disconnect-error match and adding a disconnect confirmation step. **ADR 0025 is Accepted.** **Council Review 35** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-02-council-review-35-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, four distinct agents) found no tenant-boundary or payment-routing defect, then found a pre-existing flow bug, now more visible — a paid event registration at a church that can't take payments is saved payment-pending with no way to pay (**folds into G3.0c**) — and two UX fixes, both shipped in `5493e32`: the `?stripe=` result banner no longer stays on refresh, and the fee copy now names Stripe's own processing fees. The Connect card's English-only copy joins **S13**. **6 agent claims wrong or unsupported against source** (two A3 accessibility claims contradicted by the code or a Mantine default; "all steps work end to end" with nothing run against real Stripe; an unevidenced competitor matrix; G3.0 misattributed to the wrong merge commit; a dead-code branch labelled Critical instead of Low) — see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory. **New owner action O7:** the platform's own Stripe Connect setup plus one test-mode run with a real connected account, before live giving is announced — no test so far exercises real Stripe. **Since G3.0 merged, remaining Must work drops by 1 row and 1 day: remaining Must work is now 16 rows, 24 factory days, against 26 working days (Oct 2 – Nov 6), about 2 days of slack** — G3.0b, estimated 3 days, shipped the same day as G3.0 (2026-10-02, both on this branch's commit history), consistent with the plan's calibrated pace running well under estimate on smaller rows; the rows still ahead (G3.0c, G3.1, G3.2, G3.3, G5.1, T1a) are larger and less certain, so the cut line still applies at every milestone. **Verified (re-run by the Documenter):** `tsc`; `npm run lint` (0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated warning); `npm run test:surfaces` OK (117 pages, 17 routes, 31 actions); `npx vitest run` (**2,062 tests / 177 files**, then 7/7 for the card step after the fix); `npm run test:db` (**86 tests / 14 files**); `npm run lint:migrations` PASS; `npm run build` compiled. A Back button returns to the amount/fund step, cancelling the pending gift; `cancelPendingDonationAction` cancels the PaymentIntent at Stripe and marks the gift cancelled, matched by both ids plus church plus pending, a named gift only by its own giver. New dependencies `@stripe/stripe-js` and `@stripe/react-stripe-js` (Stripe's own libraries, no new audit findings). **Council Review 34** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-02-council-review-34-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, four distinct agents) confirmed the UI and flow meet the definition of done, then found and fixed, owner-approved, in `7f3fd90`: **a latent bug that meant no live PaymentIntent could ever be created** — both `createPaymentIntent` (donations) and `createEventRegistrationPaymentIntent` sent `automatic_payment_methods: "enabled"`, which form-encodes as a bare string Stripe rejects (it wants `automatic_payment_methods[enabled]=true`); both now send `payment_method_types[]=card` instead (cards, Apple Pay and Google Pay, all completing in-page, so no redirect return is needed) — latent because live mode had never actually run before this branch; confirm and cancel now require the session's own profile when the gift names one, so only the giver can confirm or cancel a named gift (an anonymous gift stores no profile, so the two ids are the proof); the webhook's own receipt now names the church too, matching the action's. **4 agent claims wrong against source:** `cancellation_reason: "abandoned"` called possibly invalid (it's one of Stripe's own documented values); "the error isn't announced" (Mantine's `Alert` has `role="alert"` by default); "Apple/Google Pay work today" (PaymentIntent creation was failing for everyone); "readiness 78–79" (it holds at 77 until live payments can actually start) — see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory. **The round also found, verified by the orchestrator against source rather than taken from an agent's own claim, two pre-existing gaps outside G3.0's own diff: no Stripe Connect** — `giving_page` stores an unused `stripe_account_id`, but nothing sends `Stripe-Account`, `transfer_data` or `on_behalf_of`, so every church's gifts go to the single account owning `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`; and demo-mode profile saves wrote emergency contacts to `profiles`, which has no such columns, so every demo profile save failed (now written to `profile_sensitive_fields`). **A new CI check**, `tests/database/column-references.test.ts`, checks every Supabase write (~204) and select (~397) in `app/` and `lib/` against the real schema (skips writes built from a variable payload, `.rpc()` calls and raw SQL). **Council Review 31** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-01-council-review-31-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, four distinct agents) confirmed S4 meets its definition of done, found 5 agent claims wrong or unsupported against source — including "lint passes," from an agent whose tools can't run commands — and found one more real bug, fixed in `c8a9f8c`: `importFinanceRowsAction` ignored two writes' errors, so a failed finance import could stop halfway and still report "completed"; Not more, because deliveries still depend on G5.1 (Resend) and the rest of the Week 2 safety track (S4, S6, S10) is still open. **Prior: 76/100 at Review 29** (2026-10-01, diff-scoped, four distinct agents), up from 75. **S2 (webhooks fail closed, with F4) was built and Council Review 29-clean, merged the same day as PR #166 (`6436248`):** all four delivery/payment webhooks (Stripe, SendGrid, Twilio, Resend) now reject every request while their signing secret is unset, in every environment — before this, SendGrid, Twilio and Resend accepted an unsigned request whenever their secret was unset, and Stripe only checked a signature when one was configured, so a missing env var silently meant "unverified," not "rejected." Stripe adds a 300-second replay window. **SendGrid and Twilio now verify the providers' real signature schemes — the old code had invented an HMAC scheme for both that would have rejected every genuine provider event the moment a secret was actually set, so verification had never really worked for either.** SendGrid signs `timestamp + body` with ECDSA P-256 against the dashboard's verification key; Twilio signs the exact called URL (resolved from `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`) plus the sorted POST fields with HMAC-SHA1, checked against Twilio's own published example. **F4 is fixed:** `lib/communications/webhook-events.ts` wrote delivery events, the log status, suppressions and the consent row through the request's anon client inside a webhook (no user session), so none of those writes ever saved; they now use the church-scoped admin client (ADR 0022), scoped to the church the provider's message id resolves to, and the lookup is two exact-match queries rather than a request-body-built `.or()` filter string. Twilio form bodies no longer crash `JSON.parse`. Twilio error 21610 (recipient replied STOP) is now recorded as `unsubscribed`, writing an SMS suppression, and Twilio sends now request a `StatusCallback` so Twilio reports anything back at all. Suppression reasons render in words (en/es/es-PR) instead of raw codes. e2e proves webhook writes save for the first time: a signed Resend bounce and a signed Twilio 21610 each write their own suppression, and a replayed Stripe signature is rejected. **Council Review 29** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-01-council-review-29-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, four distinct agents) found no defect in the diff — S2 meets its definition of done — and 5 agent claims wrong against source: an inferred-not-verified "message ids can't collide across churches" stated as fact, a wrong cron-secret name, a wrong "more than 999ms separation between shards" claim, and two overstated claims ("Twilio doesn't forward inbound STOP," "TCPA compliance is met," with "competitors do the same" unevidenced) — see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory. **Owner decisions (2026-10-01), all of Council Review 29's proposals approved:** new owner action **O4** (confirm the hosted webhook secrets and `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` before this deploys — without them, production webhooks reject everything and Stripe donations stop reconciling); new Must row **S11** (a church-admin "remove suppression" action, audited, sequenced before G5.1 — today only SQL undoes a suppression, and S2 makes suppressions real); new Should row **S12** (operator visibility for missing provider/webhook config); inbound STOP messages deferred to §0.5 (needs a sending-number → church mapping ChurchCore doesn't have). **Verified, re-run by the Documenter:** `npm run lint` (0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated warning); `npx vitest run` (**1,943 tests / 168 files**); `npm run test:db` (**67 passed**); `npm run test:surfaces` OK; `npm run lint:migrations` PASS; `npm run build` compiled. **No PR exists yet for this branch, so the CI `e2e` job has not run** — the implementer reports the webhook e2e spec passing 20/20 locally, not independently confirmed against CI; a draft PR is needed before a merge-readiness sign-off. Volunteer Scheduling unchanged at ~90%. Not more, because deliveries still depend on G5.1 (Resend) and the rest of the Week 2 safety track (S3, S4, S6, S10) is still open. Prior: 75/100 at Review 28 (2026-09-30, re-run as four agents after GitHub's PR #165 review), up from 74. **S1 (communication-log access alignment, F7) is merged (PR #165) and its migration is applied to the hosted database:** `communication_logs`, `communication_delivery_events` and `communication_suppressions`' **select** RLS policies now use `can_manage_communications` (church admin, pastor, secretary) instead of `can_manage_church`, matching the `/app/communications/*` page gates — a ministry leader loses the read access it let through (recipients' contact emails/phones on delivery events, and the suppression list), and a secretary gains the read access the pages already show (migration `20261001000000`). **Their insert policies are dropped entirely, not re-mapped to the same roles** — every writer (queue, send, compose, cancel, suppress, unsubscribe, the retry and scheduled crons) is server-side on the church-scoped admin client, so `authenticated` has no insert policy on any of the three tables at all; mapping insert to the readers' roles would have let a secretary fabricate logs, delivery events or suppressions through PostgREST (found by GitHub's PR #165 review). `cancelScheduledMessageAction` now actually cancels, through the church-scoped admin client (ADR 0022) with a `status = 'scheduled'` condition and a row-count check, failing ("already being sent") when it loses the race with the cron — **and the scheduled cron's own claim is now checked the same way**: it previously matched 0 rows silently (cancelled between fetch and claim, or claimed by an overlapping run) and sent the message anyway; it now skips a row when the claim changes nothing (also a PR #165 review finding). "Add Suppression" is now shown to church admins only (it rendered for every role and always failed for non-admins). The e2e sweep's `church-admin` identity is now a real church admin with no platform-admin row (`nora@graceharbor.church`) instead of `sarah@churchcoreops.app`, who is also a platform admin — so church-admin checks, including `/hq` (now `platformAdminOnly` with empty `allowedRoles`), no longer pass through the platform-admin RLS bypass. **Deferred (Council Review 28 finding 4, tracked as a new row below):** the church-admin dashboard care queue is silently empty for a real church admin, because pastoral data is pastor-only (`can_access_pastoral_data`) — it reads as "nothing to do," not "restricted." **Known, out of scope here, covered by S2:** `lib/communications/webhook-events.ts` writes delivery events and suppressions through the request (anon) client in a webhook, so those writes already fail under any policy (F4). **Process correction:** the first Council Review 28 pass ran as one combined agent; `AGENTS.md`/`improve-software.md` §0 require four distinct read-only agents, caught by GitHub's PR #165 review, not by us — re-run as four (`codebase-researcher` agents, read-only by tool access), which found four claims wrong against source in addition to the one real fix above (see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory). Volunteer Scheduling unchanged at ~90%. Not more, because deliveries still depend on G5.1 (Resend) and the rest of the Week 2 safety track (S2–S4, S6, S10) is still open. Prior: 74/100 at Review 27 (2026-09-30), up from 73. **Gap 1 (service planning) is closed and milestone M1 is met**, and **S9 is merged to `main` as PR #163.** **S5 (`/hq` role source and tenancy, plus a member self-edit lock) is built and Council Review 27-clean, pending merge:** members could set their own `role`, `church_id`, `user_id`, `membership_status`, data-rights approval columns, `is_pastoral`, `safety_clearance_date`, `merged_*`, `member_number` and `family_id` through PostgREST (the table-wide UPDATE grant plus `profiles_update_self`) — a trigger now refuses that on your own profile unless you're a church admin at that church or a platform admin (narrowed from an initial `can_manage_church` exemption, which also covered pastors and ministry leaders, per the owner's decision below); a change written by the membership-snapshot sync trigger is still allowed, so a church admin can demote themselves without a misleading error. `current_user_role()`, which gates `/hq` and every `hq_*` policy, now reads `church_memberships` instead of the member-settable (and secretary-blind) `profiles.role`, and `lib/auth.ts` no longer makes up a membership from it. **Owner decision 2026-09-30: `/hq` is ChurchCore's internal project dashboard, platform staff only** — every church's admins could previously read and edit it across all churches; this **supersedes the tracker row's original "the `hq_*` tables gain `church_id` with RLS" framing** with platform-admin-only RLS on all four `hq_*` tables instead. **Council Review 27** (`docs/reviews/2026-09-30-council-review-27-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, two combined agents) confirmed S5's core correct (no member can insert profiles, write memberships, or change a locked column; staff and server-side admin flows are unaffected), then found `/api/ai` (the HQ AI advisor) wasn't platform-gated — any signed-in member could spend Anthropic credit through it — fixed by gating it like `/hq`. **Critical, fixed ahead of formal approval as a security issue:** `erase_profile_pii`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` function executable by `anon`, trusted its caller-supplied `actor_profile_id` argument exactly like `merge_duplicate_profile` had the round before (ADR 0024's pattern) — anyone holding the public anon key could erase any member's personal data by naming a church admin's profile. A sweep of every `SECURITY DEFINER` function for the same pattern found two more `anon` could call: `refresh_profile_membership_snapshot` (rewrite any user's profile snapshot) and `generate_member_number`. Fixed in migration `20260930020000`: the actor is `auth.uid()` for `erase_profile_pii`, recorded in the audit; `anon` can no longer execute `erase_profile_pii` or `generate_member_number`; nobody can call `refresh_profile_membership_snapshot` directly. Also fixed: the self-edit lock now covers `family_id` too (a member could otherwise move into another household, then edit and check its people in). **Our own ADR 0024, written the round before, should have prompted this sweep at the time — it named the pattern but checked only the one function in hand, and the second instance sat beside it** (see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory: a ninth consecutive round with a miss in our own work). **Score: 74/100.** Volunteer Scheduling ~90%, unchanged. Not more, because deliveries still depend on G5.1 (Resend) and the rest of the Week 2 safety track (S1–S4, S6, S10) is still open. Prior: 73/100 at Review 26 (2026-09-30), up from 72. **Gap 1 (service planning) is closed and milestone M1 is met:** G1.11 merged to `main` as PR #162 with every CI check green, including all four `e2e` shards. S9 (stop the login-id/profile-id mix-up from coming back) was built and Council Review 26-clean: a lint rule bans `session.profile.id` outside audit-actor use (the six remaining uses now read `session.userId`); `resolveActiveChurchProfileId` and `resolveSessionProfileId` are the one resolver, both returning `session.churchProfileId`; the `timeZone` parameters of the church-time/blockout/token-expiry helpers are required; and `profiles.user_id`'s uniqueness resolves the `limit 1` self-policy concern from Council Review 22, pinned by a DB test. Council Review 26 (two combined agents, per its own §0) found this correct, but during synthesis also found, **outside S9's diff, a live privilege escalation**: `merge_duplicate_profile` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, executable by `anon`) compared a login id with a profile id in its admin check, so every real admin failed it while anyone who passed an admin's *login* id as the actor passed and could merge (hide) any non-staff profile — and the function had never actually worked besides (it read emergency contacts from a table they moved out of in April, and could break the profiles email uniqueness constraint). Fixed ahead of formal approval, as a security issue, in migration `20260930000000`: the actor is now `auth.uid()`, `anon`/`PUBLIC` can no longer execute it, the duplicate's sign-in moves to the kept profile instead of being stranded on the hidden one, and two profiles that each already have a sign-in are refused (owner decision 2026-09-30). Two pastoral-read audit events that still recorded a profile id as the actor now record the login id, and the remaining per-call profile lookups Council Review 26 flagged (pastor, church-admin, elders, daily desk, reviewer context) now read `session.churchProfileId` too. **S9 merged to `main` as PR #163, landing migration `20260930000000` on `main`** — still pending application to the *hosted* database (O2). Volunteer Scheduling ~90%, unchanged. Prior: 72/100 at Review 25 (2026-09-30), up from 71. G1.11 (service-plan build journey) was built and Council Review 25-clean: the journey creates a plan, adds songs (new and from the library), adds a position, hand-assigns and auto-fills, checking the database at each step, and found two real bugs, both fixed — a plan saved without a linked event could never be staffed (it now gets its own staff-only event, at creation or, for an older plan, on first assignment — the owner's 2026-09-30 decision, pulling G1.8's first half forward), and adding a position didn't raise the Unfilled count. Council Review 25's P1–P5 then closed the fixes' own gaps: the auto-event is staff-only with RSVPs off (it had been members-visible); no event is orphaned by a failed plan save or a lost assignment race; `assignVolunteerAction` and auto-fill return the new shift's real id, so Remove and Remind on a just-assigned volunteer work instead of silently doing nothing; and the page keeps an older plan's newly linked event instead of unlinking it on the next Plan Details save. Volunteer Scheduling ~90% (up from ~88% at Review 24). Prior still: 71/100 at Review 24 (2026-09-29), up from 70. G1.6 (church-timezone days) is built and Council Review 24-clean: same-day conflicts, blockouts, 30-day load and month counts already worked by ADR 0023's wall-clock convention, and every "today"/"now" comparison — the member schedule, the emailed-link schedule, blockout dates, the upcoming/past plan split, confirm-link expiry — now uses the church's own today. Volunteer Scheduling ~88% (up from ~86% at Review 20–23). 70/100 at Review 23 (2026-09-29), up from 69 once G1.5's assignment notifications worked end to end on Supabase. 69/100 at Review 22 (2026-09-29), which first re-based Review 21's 67 down to 66 — Review 21's "above 71 once S8 lands" assumption held only if online card giving could actually charge, and G3.0 (found while building S8) shows it never could — then up to 69 for S8's member self-service writes (RSVP, group join, check-in, registration, data rights). 67/100 at Review 21 (2026-09-28), re-based from 71 (Review 20) to 64 because signed-in writes had never worked (S7). R1 re-baselines it at MVP.
- **Beta testers are welcome and never gate progress.**
  - Their feedback becomes a tracker row (ID `B-n`) and is ranked like any other work.
  - It is never a precondition for starting or finishing anything.
  - Real-church usage is not an MVP criterion (decision 2026-09-26).
- **The quality bar doesn't move for the date.**
  - Every change ships its tests (`AGENTS.md`, `docs/testing.md`).
  - CI stays blocking.
  - The Council runs before every non-trivial merge.
  - Scope is cut to meet the date; quality never is.
- **Work order:** top to bottom within each week. The safety rows in a week land before that week's feature rows. S2 (webhooks fail closed, with F4) lands before G5.1 (Resend, F2), as the comms follow-ups require.
- **Estimates** are in factory days: one story through build, tests, Council review and merge. They are calibrated on Service Planning Stories 1–3, which took about 1–2 days each, Council included.
  - **Owner decision 2026-09-29** (§0.4): MVP moved to Nov 6 and 5.5 factory days cut (G1.7, G1.8, G5.2 after MVP; T3 folded into R1). Status values: `Not started`, `In progress`, `Done (#PR)`, `Deferred (reason)`. #### Week 1 (Sep 28 – Oct 2), milestone M1: close gap 1 (8 must days)

| ID | Priority | Item | Est. | Definition of done | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S7 | Must | The signed-in person's church profile id | 1.5 | `session.profile.id` is the auth (login) user id, never a church `profiles.id`, yet 51 places used it as one. So members' own schedules were empty, in-app confirm and decline and signed-in blockout dates failed, and communication-log writes violated `sent_by`'s foreign key and were lost. The session gains `churchProfileId`, each usage is classified by the column it feeds, "act as myself" paths handle a missing profile, and member shift responses no longer silently match zero rows (members have no UPDATE policy). Unit tests and member e2e journeys. Found 2026-09-28 by G1.5's journey. | Done (#158) |
| S8 | Must | Member writes vs. RLS | 2 | Every member-reachable write is audited against the RLS policies, and each one is fixed. Found by Council Review 21: giving (`donations` insert/update are management-only, and a Stripe PaymentIntent is created before the failing insert, so the PaymentIntent must come only after the row can be written), mobile check-in (no member `attendance` insert), group join (no member `group_members` insert), RSVP (the `event_rsvps` policy compares a profile id with `auth.uid()`), registration capacity (counted through an RLS-filtered read) and payments (an admin-only upsert), data-rights 0-row checks, and data-rights and donation errors returned rather than thrown. **Approach (2026-09-29):** writes that carry business rules (giving, check-in, group join, registration capacity and payments) go through a server-side admin client scoped by `church_id` after the action's own checks (ADR 0022), not new member RLS policies, so a member can't skip those rules by writing the table directly. A member-JWT DB harness (`tests/database/member-writes-rls.test.ts`) proves those direct writes stay denied. RSVP is the one RLS fix (migration `20260929000000`): own profile, own church and an RSVP-enabled event only. Journeys for giving, group join, RSVP and full-event registration in `tests/e2e/member-writes.spec.ts`. **Found while fixing:** donation confirmation trusted the client (it now requires Stripe to report the PaymentIntent `succeeded`, only from `pending`); members had no SELECT on `event_registration_settings`, and PostgREST's object-shaped `events` embed was read as `[0]`, so member registration options were always empty (the settings hold access codes, so reads moved server-side rather than opening a member SELECT). The same `[0]` fix was applied to the public registration page's read, but that alone doesn't make it work — see S10. **Council Review 22 (2026-09-29, P1–P8, `docs/reviews/2026-09-29-council-review-22-synthesis.md`):** the donation stub (`"succeeded"` with no Stripe keys) now runs only outside production or in demo mode, so a keyless production deploy can't record fake succeeded gifts; with Stripe configured, `initiateDonationAction` returns "isn't available yet" before writing a row or calling Stripe (G3.0 isn't built), and the giving page shows that notice up front with Give disabled; confirm now posts to the GL through a shared `lib/stripe/donation-completion.ts` module, whichever of confirm or the webhook wins the `pending → succeeded` update; receipt HTML (`donor_name`, `fund_designation`) is escaped; registration re-enforces `events.visibility`, which the settings read had bypassed; household-profile reads and the registration insert move to the scoped admin client so a household member's row can be read back; check-in and registration errors no longer leak raw database text; and two member writes S8's own audit missed are fixed — a re-submitted pending change request (was a 0-row update that silently dropped the new changes) and a second save of notification preferences (was a unique-key error, now upserts on `church_id, profile_id`). Must land before G1.5 resumes and before G3.1. | Done (#159, Council Review 22) |
| S9 | Must | Stop the login-id / profile-id mix-up from coming back | 0.5 | Rename the session's `profile.id` so it reads as the login id. A lint rule bans `session.profile.id` in `app/` and `lib/` outside audit-actor use. One resolver instead of `churchProfileId` plus `resolveActiveChurchProfileId`. Member updates check their row count. **Widened (Council Review 22):** self policies that pick a profile with `where user_id = auth.uid() limit 1` and no church filter (`event_registrations_select_own`, `notification_preferences_*`, `consent_logs`) match by church too — a login with profiles in more than one church can otherwise read or write against the wrong one's row. **Widened (Council Review 24):** the `timeZone` parameters `lib/church-time.ts`'s helpers and the blockout/schedule loaders take are optional today, so a future caller that omits one silently gets UTC instead of the church's zone — make them required as one of this row's regression guards. **DoD interpretation (Council Review 26):** "rename" is met by a session-scoped lint rule plus the six audit-actor uses reading `session.userId` (a real TypeScript rename of the field was judged out of scope for the estimate); "one resolver" is met by every helper (`resolveActiveChurchProfileId`, `resolveSessionProfileId`, `requirePastorProfileContext`, `requireChurchAdminProfileContext`, and the per-call lookups in pastor/church-admin/elders/daily-desk/reviewer contexts) returning `session.churchProfileId`; the `limit 1` self-policy concern is resolved because `profiles.user_id` is unique (a login has exactly one profile), pinned by a DB test that a second profile for the same login is rejected by `profiles_user_id_uidx`. **Shipped (`3ab51df`):** as above. **Council Review 26** (`docs/reviews/2026-09-30-council-review-26-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, two combined agents): S9's changes confirmed correct, but found two clauses only partial — about 9 other per-call profile lookups still ran their own query (several without a `merged_at` filter), and the lint selector was narrower/broader than intended in different ways (missed destructuring, over-matched any `x.profile.id`) — plus, **outside the diff**, a live privilege escalation in `merge_duplicate_profile` (see the MVP readiness history and ADR 0024). **Council Review 26 fixes (`e01f2d4`, owner-approved P1–P5):** `merge_duplicate_profile` takes its actor from `auth.uid()`, revokes `anon`/`PUBLIC` execute, merges sensitive fields from their real table, releases the kept email conflict, deactivates memberships by login id, moves the duplicate's sign-in to the kept profile, and refuses merging two profiles that each already have one (migration `20260930000000`, DB tests in `tests/database/profile-merge-auth.test.ts`); the two remaining profile-id audit actors now read the login id; the remaining per-call lookups read `session.churchProfileId`; the lint rule is scoped to sessions and also catches destructuring; the demo profile save returns an error result instead of throwing; stale comments fixed. **Not independently re-verified by a further Council round for S9 itself** — Review 26's synthesis approved these as proposed prompts (P1–P5) and the owner approved them; Council Review 27 audited the follow-on S5 branch, not this fix commit. **Merged to `main` as PR #163 (2026-09-30).** | Done (#163) |
| G1.4 | Must | Blockout dates (FS3-1) | 1.5 | A volunteer adds and removes unavailable dates from `/app/member/schedule`. An admin adds and removes them from the volunteer directory. The planner's "Unavailable that day" and the assign modal honor them. Unit, action, DB and journey tests. | Done (#156) |
| G1.5 | Must | Assignment notifications (FS3-2) | 1.5 | Assigning a volunteer, whether by hand or by auto-fill, sends an email (or SMS, if that's their preference) with the accept/decline link, through the existing communications pipeline, with consent and suppression respected. A decline shows the admin the top replacement suggestions. **Every assignment (manual and auto-fill) creates the shift's own confirmation token, valid until the shift date plus 7 days** (today only a manual reminder creates one, with a fixed 14-day expiry; Council Review 20). The roster shows the reason when an assigned volunteer has blocked the date. Tests, including the journey: assign → volunteer declines → admin sees replacements. **Shipped (Council Review 23, `feat/assignment-notifications-g1-5`, `83b7a2d`/`a99cfea`/`34535c1`):** manual and auto-fill assignments both message the volunteer by email or SMS per preference, with a per-shift token valid to the service date + 7 days; a decline surfaces ranked replacement suggestions one click away; the roster shows the blockout reason. **P1–P7 fixes:** P1 closed a real token exposure (any member could read another volunteer's confirm token through PostgREST — migration `20260929010000` plus an admin-client-scoped read/write with a row-count check); P2 falls back to email when a volunteer prefers SMS but never opted in, and tells the admin why; P3 makes the email/SMS/Resend stubs fail closed in production (`lib/stub-mode.ts`, shared with Review 22's Stripe stub); P4 adds one app-URL resolver (`lib/app-url.ts`) so a production deploy without `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` skips the send instead of mailing a dead `localhost` link; P5 names the church in the message, trims SMS to about two segments, shows yellow for a skipped/failed outcome, fixes the confirm page's stale "14 days" copy, adds Replace for a volunteer who blocked the date, and excludes the person who just declined from that date's replacement suggestions; P6 stops Remind's double-send and false reminder count; P7 gives the unit tests distinct login/profile ids with `sent_by` asserted, adds a DB test for the token grant, and fixes the list-page manifest entry. | Done (#160) |
| G1.6 | Must | Church-timezone days (FS3-4) | 0.5 | Same-day conflicts, blockout dates, 30-day load and month counts use `churches.timezone`, not UTC. Test: an evening service in UTC−4 is on the right day. **DoD amendment (Council Review 24):** the same-day conflict, 30-day load and month-count clauses were already met by ADR 0023's wall-clock convention (a shift's stored wall-clock time already reads back as its own local day) — only the "today"/"now" comparisons needed converting to the church's zone. **Shipped (`ad09cab`):** `lib/church-time.ts` (the church's today; the real instant a local day starts, DST-safe); church-local today for shift responses, blockout add/remove/list (member, token link, admin), the member schedule, and the upcoming/past plan split; confirm links expire at local midnight. ADR 0023 records the convention. **Council Review 24 fixes (`2cca5bf`):** the emailed-link schedule (`getPublicVolunteerScheduleByToken`) had the same UTC bug G1.6 fixed in the signed-in schedule — it now lists from the church's today too; the admin plan list re-split upcoming/past in UTC on the client — the page now passes the church's today down; `church-time.ts` fixed for the day a DST transition skips midnight (Santiago, Havana) and no longer throws on a malformed day; the convention is enforced (`assignVolunteerAction` refuses an offset-bearing shift time, church settings accept only a valid IANA zone, run-of-service times display wall-clock); the emailed link refuses a shift that already happened, with a row-count check; regression tests for every fixed path at an evening UTC−4 clock; ADR 0023 corrected (burnout, last-served, the DB session-TimeZone dependency). | Done (#161) |
| G1.11 | Must | Service-planning build journey (Gap 1 CI clause) | 0.5 | An e2e journey creates a service plan, adds songs from the library, assigns volunteers by hand and by auto-fill. Closes Gap 1's CI-journey clause — `service-plan-rotation.spec.ts` covers auto-fill, confirm, decline, blockout-skip and notify, but uses a seeded plan and never exercises plan creation or the song library through the UI (found by Council Review 24 Agent 4, approved by the owner 2026-09-29/30 as the next item, right after G1.6 merges and before S9; no DoD amendment). **Shipped (`1233705`):** the journey creates a plan, adds a song not in the library (creating it) and again from the library, adds a position, hand-assigns and auto-fills, asserting the database at each step. A plan now always gets its event — a new plan at creation, an older plan on its first assignment (owner decision 2026-09-30, pulling G1.8's first half forward) — since an event-less plan could never be staffed; adding a position now raises the Unfilled count without a reload. **Council Review 25 fixes (`b867174`):** the auto-event is staff-only (`visibility: leaders`, RSVPs off) instead of members-visible; no orphan event survives a failed plan insert or a lost assignment race, and `zonedTimeToInstant` refuses impossible dates/times; `assignVolunteerAction` and auto-fill return the real shift id so Remove/Remind on a just-assigned volunteer actually work (one waiver retired); the page keeps an older plan's newly linked event on the next Plan Details save instead of unlinking it; the New Plan form explains the staff-only event. **Merged to `main` as PR #162** with every CI check green, including all four `e2e` shards — Gap 1 (service planning) is closed and milestone **M1** is met. | Done (#162) |
| G1.7 | Must | Rehearsal scheduling (Story 4) | 1.5 | A plan can have one or more rehearsals (date, time, location, which positions attend). Assigned volunteers see them on their schedule and in the calendar. Assignment notifications include them. Journey test. | Deferred (owner decision 2026-09-29: MVP moved to Nov 6 and ~5.5 days cut, see §0.4; Gap 1 closes on planning, assignment, notification and response without rehearsals) |

| S5 | Must | `/hq` role source and tenancy, and the profile self-edit lock | 1 | `/hq` authorizes from membership roles, like the rest of the app. The `hq_*` tables gain `church_id` with RLS. The profile-role drift found at Council Review 18 is traced and fixed. **Council Review 21 adds a privilege escalation:** members can update their own `profiles.role`, `church_id`, `user_id` and `membership_status` (`profiles_update_self` plus column grants), and `current_user_role()`, which gates `/hq` and every `hq_*` policy, trusts `profiles.role`. So those columns are locked against self-edits, `current_user_role()` reads `church_memberships`, and `lib/auth.ts` drops its `profiles.role` membership fallback. **Widened (Council Review 22):** the same column lock also covers the data-rights approval columns (`data_delete_approved_at`/`_by`), `is_pastoral` and `safety_clearance_date` — `profiles_update_self` has no column limits today, so a member can set any of these on their own row through PostgREST. Tests, including a DB test that a member can't change their own role or these columns. **Owner decision 2026-09-30, supersedes this row's original tenancy framing:** `/hq` is ChurchCore's internal project dashboard, not a church feature — it is **platform-staff only**, not per-church `hq_*` tenancy with `church_id`/RLS as originally scoped. **Shipped (`43d0305`):** a trigger (`profiles_block_protected_self_edit`) refuses a change to `role`, `church_id`, `user_id`, `membership_status`, the data-rights approval columns, `is_pastoral`, `safety_clearance_date`, `merged_into_profile_id`, `merged_at` or `member_number` on your own profile unless you manage the church there or are a platform admin; `current_user_role()` reads `church_memberships`; `/hq`'s four tables (`hq_tasks`, `hq_risks`, `hq_decisions`, `hq_sessions`) are RLS-gated to `is_platform_admin`. Migration `20260930010000`; DB tests for each (`tests/database/profiles-self-edit-lock.test.ts`). **Council Review 27** (`docs/reviews/2026-09-30-council-review-27-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, two combined agents) confirmed the core correct (no member can insert a profile, write a membership, or change a locked column; staff and server-side admin flows unaffected), then found: **Critical, fixed ahead of formal approval as a security issue** — `erase_profile_pii` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, executable by `anon`) trusted its caller-supplied `actor_profile_id` argument, the same flaw ADR 0024 named in `merge_duplicate_profile` the round before; anyone holding the public anon key could erase any member's personal data by naming a church admin's profile. A sweep of every `SECURITY DEFINER` function for the same pattern found two more callable by `anon`: `refresh_profile_membership_snapshot` and `generate_member_number`. **Fixed in migration `20260930020000`:** `erase_profile_pii`'s actor is `auth.uid()`, recorded in the audit; `anon` can no longer execute `erase_profile_pii` or `generate_member_number`; nobody can call `refresh_profile_membership_snapshot` directly. **High, fixed:** `family_id` wasn't locked — a member could move into another household, then edit and check its people in; now locked with the other protected columns. **Medium, fixed:** `/api/ai` (the HQ AI advisor) checked only that the caller was signed in, so any member could spend Anthropic credit and its `hq_sessions` insert failed silently; now gated to `is_platform_admin`, like `/hq`. **Narrowed per owner decision 2026-09-30:** the self-edit exemption had used `can_manage_church`, which includes pastor and ministry_leader, letting them self-set `safety_clearance_date`, the data-rights approval columns and `is_pastoral` — now only church admins and platform admins are exempt; a change written by the membership-snapshot sync trigger is still allowed, fixing a church admin's self-demotion showing a misleading error. Clean-ups: the stale `KNOWN_BUGS["secretary /hq"]` sweep entry and `/hq`'s dead role branches removed. All DB, route and e2e tests for each. **Council Review 27's own correction:** our own ADR 0024, written the round before, named this exact pattern but checked only the function in hand — the second instance (`erase_profile_pii`) sat beside it; see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory. **Not independently re-verified by a further Council round** — these are the fixes Council Review 27 itself proposed and the owner approved; no later round has re-audited commit `dcd6d4d`. | Done — merged as PR #164 (`dc0566c`), 2026-09-30 (status corrected 2026-10-01; this row read "pending merge" after the PR had already merged) |

This row's pure church-admin fixture is the fix: once it exists, the `/hq` manifest entry should be corrected to platform-admin-only and re-verified it now denies a church-admin-only identity. **Shipped (`3a704f4`):** migration `20261001000000` moves `communication_logs`' select and insert policies to `can_manage_communications`.
