DB tests in `tests/database/communication-logs-access.test.ts`. **Council Review 28** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-01-council-review-28-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, one combined agent) confirmed the fix and the nora fixture correct, then found, still on `can_manage_church` and not aligned to the pages: `communication_delivery_events` (select/insert — a secretary's message analytics showed zeros; a ministry leader could read recipients' contact details) and `communication_suppressions` (select/insert — a ministry leader could read and add suppressions on any member's email/phone; a secretary saw none); also confirmed `cancelScheduledMessageAction` was a silent no-op, since `communication_logs` has no UPDATE policy and the action used the user's own client. **Fixed in the same migration and `4477c5b` (owner-approved fixes 1, 2, 3 and 5; fix 4 deferred, new row below):** `communication_delivery_events` and `communication_suppressions` move reads to `can_manage_communications` too; `cancelScheduledMessageAction` now cancels through the church-scoped admin client (ADR 0022) with a `status = 'scheduled'` condition and a row-count check, failing instead of silently succeeding when it loses the race with the cron; stale sarah comments and the control-plane sweep skip are fixed; the validator's error names `platformAdminOnly`. **Out of scope, tracked as S2:** `lib/communications/webhook-events.ts` writes delivery events and suppressions through the request (anon) client in a webhook, so those writes already fail under any RLS policy (F4). **PR #165 opened; GitHub's automated review found three more things, fixed in `bd975a2`:** the scheduled cron's own claim (`scheduled` → `sending`) was unchecked, so a message cancelled between the cron's fetch and its claim, or claimed by an overlapping run, was still sent — the cron now skips a row when the claim changes 0 rows, the same guard as the cancel action; mapping insert to the readers' roles would have let a secretary fabricate delivery events or suppressions through PostgREST, so **`authenticated` now gets no insert policy at all** on any of the three tables — every writer, including `suppressContactAction` (church-admin only), moves to the church-scoped admin client, and a DB test pins that no role, pastor included, can insert directly; the validator's `platformAdminOnly` exemption gained its own test. **The Council format itself was wrong, and GitHub's review caught it too:** `AGENTS.md`/`improve-software.md` §0 require four distinct read-only audit agents; | S2 | Must | Webhooks fail closed (with F4) | 1 | All four delivery and payment webhooks (Stripe, SendGrid, Twilio, Resend) reject requests when their signing secret is unset, in every environment. Stripe enforces a replay window. The F4 webhook log lookup is verified: suppressions from bounces and STOPs are actually written. Route tests for each. **Shipped (`26db13c` build, `d57e7c0` Council Review 29 fixes):** all four webhooks fail closed — before this, SendGrid, Twilio and Resend accepted an unsigned request whenever their secret was unset, and Stripe only checked a signature when a secret existed. Stripe adds a 300-second replay window (`lib/stripe/webhook-signature.ts`). **SendGrid and Twilio now verify the providers' real signature schemes — the old code used an invented HMAC for both, which would have rejected every genuine provider event once a secret was actually configured, so verification had never really worked for either.** SendGrid signs `timestamp + body` with ECDSA P-256 against the dashboard's verification key; Twilio signs the exact called URL (resolved from `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`, since the request's own host isn't the public one behind a proxy) plus the sorted POST fields with HMAC-SHA1, checked against Twilio's own published example. **F4 fixed:** `lib/communications/webhook-events.ts` wrote delivery events, log status, suppressions and the consent row through the anon request client inside a webhook (no session), so none of it saved; writes now use the church-scoped admin client (ADR 0022), scoped to the church of the log the provider's message id resolves to. The message-id lookup is two exact-match queries, not a request-built `.or()` filter string. Twilio form bodies no longer crash `JSON.parse`. Twilio error 21610 (recipient replied STOP) is recorded as `unsubscribed`, writing an SMS suppression; Twilio sends now request a `StatusCallback` so Twilio reports anything back at all. Suppression reasons render in words (en/es/es-PR) instead of raw codes. e2e: a signed Resend bounce and a signed Twilio 21610 each write their suppression (the first proof any webhook write actually saves), and a replayed Stripe signature is rejected. **Council Review 29** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-01-council-review-29-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, four distinct agents) found no defect in the diff — S2 meets its definition of done — and 5 agent claims wrong against source (see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory). **Owner decisions (2026-10-01):** new owner action O4 (confirm the hosted webhook secrets and `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` before deploy); new Must row **S11** (church-admin remove-suppression action, sequenced before G5.1); new Should row **S12** (operator visibility for missing provider/webhook config); inbound STOP messages deferred to §0.5. **Verified:** `npm run lint` (0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated warning); `npx vitest run` (**1,943 tests / 168 files**); `npm run test:db` (**67 passed**); `npm run test:surfaces` OK; `npm run lint:migrations` PASS; `npm run build` compiled — all re-run by the Documenter. **No PR exists yet for this branch, so the CI `e2e` job has not run;** the implementer reports the webhook e2e spec passing 20/20 locally, not independently confirmed against CI. | Done — merged as PR #166 (`6436248`), 2026-10-01; PR review fixes `7de95c3` (retries resume, Twilio dedupe, SendGrid batches). No migrations. |

DB test that an anonymous client can't insert directly. **Shipped (`47e6e8d`):** migration `20261002000000` drops `event_registrations_public_insert`. The visitor page reads settings, registration counts and form fields server-side with the church-scoped admin client (ADR 0022), scoped to public events open for registration — before, it read through the anon client and showed no events, even after S8's `[0]`-embed read-shape fix. `submitPublicEventRegistrationAction` writes with the admin client only after its own visibility, deadline, capacity and waitlist checks; keeps only the event's own custom fields and enforces its required ones; shows generic errors instead of raw database text; undoes a paid registration whose payment row failed to write, instead of leaving the visitor believing they'd registered (resolves the Council Review 22 "payment-row upsert fails silently" §0.5 item below); and rate-limits each IP to 10 registrations/minute (in-memory per instance). DB test in `tests/database/event-registrations-insert.test.ts`; journey in `tests/e2e/public-event-registration.spec.ts`. **Council Review 33** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-02-council-review-33-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, four distinct agents) confirmed S10 meets its definition of done and the Week 2 safety track is complete with it, then found and fixed on this branch (`30e9046`, owner-approved): migration `20261002010000` narrows `event_registration_payments`' member read policy to a member's own registrations only (any member could previously read every registration payment in their church, with no member screen that needed it) and drops `account_requests`' direct anon-insert policy, leaving `submit_account_request` (`SECURITY DEFINER`) the only way in; checkbox custom fields are real, required checkboxes instead of toggle Buttons with no checkbox role or required marker — following that UX finding led to a real server bug, an unchecked required checkbox arriving as `false` and passing the required check, fixed with a test; event times show in the church's own time zone, named, instead of the visitor's unlabeled browser zone. A schema sweep found no other unconditional write policy. **7 agent claims wrong or overstated against source** (church list called "via the admin client" instead of the `list_portal_churches` RPC; the admin client said to "respect" RLS; "27 test cases" when the action has 11; S6 called "pending" when merged as #169; Gap 1's close date given as Oct 2 instead of Sep 30; S11 called a Should row when it's Must; "the rate limit prevents spam" overstated) — see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory. **Owner decisions (2026-10-02):** translating the public registration page and panel is added to **S13**; the event-registration capacity race (count-then-insert, not atomic) and the per-instance rate limiter are noted under **S14**, to revisit before scale. **With S10, the Week 2 production-safety track (S1–S6, S9, S10) is complete — milestone M2 is met, ahead of the Oct 9 date.** **Score:** MVP readiness holds at **77/100** (Council Review 33, 2026-10-02) — the safety track closes, but no competitive gap moves. **Verified (re-run by the Documenter):** `npm run lint` (0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated warning); `npx vitest run` (**1,991 tests / 170 files**); `npm run test:db` (**80 passed**); `npm run test:surfaces` OK; `npm run lint:migrations` PASS; `npm run build` compiled. | G3.0 | Must | Card form for online giving (found in S8) | 1 | Stripe Elements collects the card and confirms the PaymentIntent in the browser. Until now, with Stripe configured, the giving page could create a PaymentIntent but had no form to pay it, so S8 makes it say online card giving isn't available and that nothing was charged. `confirmDonationAction` runs after `stripe.confirmPayment` succeeds; the webhook remains the source of truth. Also removes S8's `onlineGivingMode()` "unavailable" guard (P2, Council Review 22) in favor of a "live" mode, and cancels the PaymentIntent for a gift the member abandons instead of leaving it orphaned. **Shipped (`f9a054a`):** the Give drawer's card step (`components/portal/donation-card-step.tsx`) uses Stripe Elements' Payment Element and `confirmPayment` with `redirect: "if_required"` — a declined card shows Stripe's own message and the member can retry; on success `confirmDonationAction` checks the status with Stripe before recording the gift, and the webhook stays the source of truth; a payment still processing is left to the webhook. `onlineGivingMode()` gains a `"live"` mode (both Stripe keys set); `"unavailable"` now means only the publishable key is missing. A Back button returns to the amount/fund step, cancelling the pending gift; `cancelPendingDonationAction` cancels the PaymentIntent at Stripe and marks the gift cancelled, matched by both ids plus church plus pending. | G3.0b | Must | Stripe Connect: per-church payment routing (found in G3.0) | 3 | Each church connects its own Stripe account (Standard accounts, Account Links or OAuth onboarding), storing the account id on `giving_page` (the `stripe_account_id` column already exists, unused until now). Donation and event-registration PaymentIntents are created with the `Stripe-Account` header as **direct charges** on the church's own account (the church is merchant of record; no platform fee); Stripe.js loads with `stripeAccount` set to the church's account; Connect webhooks are routed by `event.account` and checked against the church. Online giving reads "not set up" until a church has connected. **Owner decision 2026-10-02 (Council Review 34):** Standard accounts plus direct charges, built now, right after G3.0 and before G3.1 — recurring gifts (G3.1) must also run on each church's own account, so this has to land first. Today every church's gifts go to the single account owning `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`; the `giving_page.stripe_account_id` column and a code comment referencing "the church's connected Stripe account" both predate any code that actually uses it. An ADR records the choice when this row ships. Tests. **Shipped (`f45b7e8`):** `church_payment_accounts` (migration `20261003000000`) holds one Stripe account per church — church-admin read-only RLS scoped to their own church, no anon access, written only by the server; `donations` and `event_registration_payments` each gain a `stripe_account_id` column so confirm, cancel, refunds and subscription cancels reach the right account later, even after a church reconnects a different one. `/api/stripe/connect/start` and `/callback` are church-admin only; the OAuth `state` is HMAC-signed, expires in 15 minutes, and binds the callback to the church and admin who started it. Every live Stripe call carries `Stripe-Account`; Stripe.js loads on the church's own account; a live call made without one throws instead of charging the platform. The webhook accepts the Connect endpoint's secret, scopes a connected event to the account's church (ignoring one naming another church or from an unlinked account), and handles `account.updated` and `account.application.deauthorized`. `disconnectStripeAccountAction` revokes access at Stripe first, asks for confirmation, and tolerates only Stripe's exact "not connected" error. **Found while building, fixed on the branch (not a Council finding):** `stripeRequest` misread Connect OAuth's flat error shape (`{ error: "invalid_client", error_description }` vs the API's nested `{ error: { message } }`), so every OAuth failure read as a generic "Stripe 401" — disconnecting an account the church had already revoked at Stripe could never succeed; the tolerated-error match was narrowed from a loose `/invalid/` (which would have wrongly marked a still-connected church disconnected on "Invalid API Key") to Stripe's exact message; disconnect gained a confirmation step. **ADR 0025 is Accepted**, with implementation notes added after this review. **Council Review 35** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-02-council-review-35-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, four distinct agents) found no tenant-boundary or payment-routing defect, then found: a pre-existing flow bug, now more visible — a paid event registration at a church that can't take payments is saved payment-pending with no way to pay (**folds into G3.0c**, below); the `?stripe=` result banner stayed on refresh (**fixed, `5493e32`**: the card drops the query parameter once it's shown the message); the fee copy didn't mention Stripe's own processing fees (**fixed, same commit**: "ChurchCore takes no fee; Stripe's standard processing fees apply"); the card is English-only (**joins S13**). **6 agent claims wrong or unsupported against source:** two A3 accessibility claims contradicted by the code or a library default (the status badges called colour-only, when each has distinct text; the alerts said to lack `role="alert"`, when Mantine's `Alert` defaults to it); "all steps work end to end" stated as fact with nothing run against real Stripe; an unevidenced competitor matrix; G3.0 misattributed to the wrong merge commit; a dead-code branch (the local-SQL refund path, unreachable under the Supabase-only mandate) labelled Critical instead of Low — see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory. **New owner action O7:** the platform's own Stripe Connect setup (`STRIPE_CONNECT_CLIENT_ID`, the OAuth redirect registered at Stripe, `NEXT_PUBLIC_STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, a Connect webhook endpoint and its `STRIPE_CONNECT_WEBHOOK_SECRET`) plus one Stripe test-mode run with a real connected account, before live giving is announced — no test so far exercises real Stripe. **Score:** MVP readiness **79/100** (Council Review 35, 2026-10-02), up from 77. **Verified (re-run by the Documenter):** `tsc`; `npm run lint` (0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated warning); `npm run test:surfaces` OK (117 pages, 17 routes, 31 actions); `npx vitest run` (**2,062 tests / 177 files**, then 7/7 for the card step after the fix); `npm run test:db` (**86 tests / 14 files**); `npm run lint:migrations` PASS; `npm run build` compiled. Targeted e2e (`api-session-routes`, `api-webhooks`, `church-admin-readiness`): 52/52. The broader member-writes/member-mobile-foundation/page-role-sweep run: 731 passed, 2 failed and 4 flaky, all 10-second "denied" timeouts on unrelated pages (a different page each run), each passing in isolation (34/34) — known local load flakes; CI is the judge. This branch carries one new migration (`20261003000000`), applied locally only — the hosted push happens after merge, dry run first. **No PR exists yet for this branch, so the CI `e2e` job has not run** — not independently confirmed; a draft PR is needed before this is treated as merge-ready. **Merged 2026-10-02 as PR #174 (`d51f312`); hosted migration `20261003000000` applied the same day (dry run first).** GitHub's PR review then found five more issues after Council Review 35 passed clean, all fixed before merge: a failure after Stripe authorized an account could leave access that no church was linked to (the callback now revokes it, unless another church is connected to that account); a late or retried deauthorization webhook could disconnect a church that had since reconnected (account events now apply only to a link made at or before the event); the start route returned a 500, not `not_configured`, without the platform secret key; the migration and ADR had promised refunds and cancels on a disconnected account, which revoked access makes impossible (corrected); a stale note. **This Documenter close-out found the row's own status here had not been updated after the merge** — it still read "pending merge" — and corrected the remaining-Must-work recompute below accordingly (§0.1). | Done (#174), 2026-10-02 |

Sequenced after G3.0b, so it's built against connected accounts rather than the single-account path. Tests, including the declined-card path. Owner-approved 2026-10-02 (Council Review 34). **Widened (Council Review 35, owner decision 2026-10-02):** also refuses a paid registration with a clear message when the church can't take payments, and stops swallowing the Stripe error, at the same five call sites Agent 2 found (`app/portal/actions.ts:396-414`; `app/app/member-actions.ts:655-673` and `:847-860`; `app/app/church-admin-actions.ts:1547-1561` and `:1654-1665`) — pre-existing (every caller already swallowed `createEventRegistrationPaymentIntent`'s error), but Connect makes an unconnected church the normal case rather than a misconfiguration. it now creates no PaymentIntent, records the payment as due, and tells the admin to collect it in person — an admin can always add a walk-in, even at an unconnected church, which supersedes this row's literal refuse-everywhere wording for the admin path only. **Found by the orchestrator while building, fixed:** `registerForEventAction`, a `"use server"` export, had no session or role check — RLS (`can_manage_church`) was its only gate; it now has the event page's own gate (church admin, pastor, in the session's church). **Two of the five call sites this row originally listed are dead local-SQL branches** (`shouldUseLocalTenantFallback()` always returns `false`), left untouched under the Supabase-only mandate. **Council Review 36** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-02-council-review-36-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, four distinct agents) confirmed G3.0c meets its row — no agent found a security or tenant defect, and four real accessibility/copy findings were fixed on the branch — then fixed, owner-approved, in `50dc819`: the payment step says up front that closing the window, or choosing Cancel registration, cancels the registration and that the registrant won't be charged; "the secretary role isn't tested as denied" (it is — the admin test loops over member, secretary and ministry-leader); "the `size=\"lg\"` modal isn't responsive" (Mantine's modal content has `max-width: 100%`, so it shrinks on phones); "the old admin path never created a PaymentIntent" (it did, and discarded the client secret); "registration receipts not built" (half-right — Stripe's own `receipt_email` is set on the PaymentIntent); new Should row **S17** tracks letting a registrant resume and pay an unpaid registration from a link in the confirmation email (about 1 day, after MVP Must work — today, closing the payment step cancels the registration and the registrant must start over); | G3.2 | Must | Installments from Stripe webhooks | 2.5 | `invoice.paid`, `invoice.payment_failed` and `customer.subscription.updated` record each installment as a donation, send its receipt, and post it to the ledger through the existing finance posting. A failed payment notifies the donor. Webhook and finance tests. **Widened (owner decision 2026-10-01, Council Review 31, +0.5 day):** the Stripe webhook route is not retry-safe today — it returns 200 on a handler error, so Stripe never retries, and the donation path's `pending → succeeded` flip happens first, so a later failure (ledger post, receipt) is never retried even on a 5xx. This row's definition of done now also requires: a 5xx response on handler failure so Stripe retries; every step repeatable (idempotent) on retry; the completion marker written last, after every other write succeeds; and a retry e2e. **Est. corrected 2 → 2.5** (Council Review 38 Documenter close-out): the +0.5 day widening above had only ever been stated in this row's prose, never applied to the Est. column, so every prior remaining-Must-work total understated this row by half a day. **Shipped together with G3.1** (see its row for the build): `invoice.paid` records each installment as a donation keyed by its invoice (so a Stripe retry can't double-record it) and completes it through a shared `completeDonation` (the webhook, the member confirm and the public stub path all use it — succeeded, then the ledger post, repeatable, a half-written journal replaced, then the receipt, claimed before sending and released if refused, then `completed_at`, written last); `invoice.payment_failed` records the failure, tells the donor once, and marks the gift past due; `customer.subscription.updated`/`deleted` sync the gift, ordered by event time, and a cancelled gift never comes back. The webhook route now answers 5xx on a handler failure, so Stripe retries, instead of 200, closing this row's widened definition of done. **Found by the orchestrator, fixed:** `cancelStripeSubscription` called `POST /v1/subscriptions/:id/cancel`, an endpoint that doesn't exist for subscriptions (Stripe cancels one with `DELETE /v1/subscriptions/:id`) — pre-existing, the same class as Council Review 34's malformed PaymentIntent parameter, fixed (`8149031`) with a test pinning the request. **Fixed after Council (`2d2a522`):** a subscription created before a later failure is now cancelled at Stripe too; a gift whose card was never confirmed stays incomplete instead of acting like a real gift; a retry e2e proves a signed `invoice.paid` resumes an installment an earlier attempt left half-finished with no second receipt, and a repeated delivery records nothing twice. **Council Review 38** confirmed G3.2 meets its widened row. **Score:** MVP readiness **82/100**, up from 80 — see the MVP readiness history. | Done (#177), 2026-10-03 (migration `20261004000000` applied to hosted Supabase 2026-10-03) |
| G3.3 | Must | Year-end giving statements | 2 | An admin generates per-donor statements for a date range (default: last calendar year): a PDF with the church header, gifts and the tax-deductibility wording. Batch email to donors, with consent respected. Members download their own from `/app/member/giving`. Tests and a journey. **Widened (Council Review 36, owner decision 2026-10-02):** also covers ChurchCore's own receipt for a paid event registration — today only Stripe's own receipt is sent, via the PaymentIntent's `receipt_email`, if the church enables Stripe receipts; ChurchCore sends none. Not re-estimated. **2026-10-04 (owner): the registration-receipt widening is split out into G3.3b below; G3.3 covers statements only.** **Shipped (`60d912b` build, `55a2418` Council Review 40 fixes):** admin preview, named-gift PDF, idempotent batch email (claim-before-send, migration `20261005000000`), member self-download; anonymity holds against staff end to end (owner decision 2026-10-04). ### 0.4 Cut line

On each milestone date, compare the Must rows due by then against their status.
- **More than two factory days behind:** Stretch rows move to "Deferred to after MVP", in this order:
  1. G3.4 pledges
  2. G4.3 guided migration flow
  3. G2.3 offline schedule
  4. G1.10 recurring blockouts
  5. G1.9 transactional assign
- **Still behind after that:** the owner decides whether to move the date or cut a Must row. That decision is recorded here with its date.
- **Must rows are never silently dropped.**

**Decisions recorded here:**
- **2026-09-29 (owner), date and scope.** With 33 Must days left against 23 working days, and each fix finding more work (7.5 days added between Sep 26 and Sep 29), the owner moved MVP one week to **Friday, November 6** and cut about 5.5 days: G1.7 rehearsals, G1.8 plan-requires-event and G5.2 accounting export move after MVP (§0.5), and T3 is folded into R1, so there is one full Testing Council run, against the release candidate. The safety track and giving rows are untouched. Must work left after the decision: 27.5 factory days against 28 working days. Chosen over keeping Oct 30 with a ~10-day cut (which would have left gaps 2 and 4 only partly closed) and over Nov 13 with full scope.

### 0.5 Deferred to after MVP

Each item was triaged on 2026-09-26 from the Council reviews and the plan history. Each is deliberately out of MVP scope.

| Item | Source | Why it's after MVP |
|---|---|---|
| Multi-week auto-scheduling, household "schedule with", volunteer-set preferred frequency (FS3-6) | Review 19 | Competitive long tail. The planner is complete without them. |
| Share the burnout rule between `lib/burnout-calculator.ts` and the planner (FS3-5) | Review 19 | Duplicated constant. Behavior is already consistent, with the pool check the stricter one. |
| DLQ visibility: Sentry reporting, a failure count in the cron response, a DLQ view (F3) | Reviews 16–17 | Operational visibility. Failures are already recorded in `communication_dlq`. |
| Native-speaker review of the `es`/`es-PR` catalogs, and i18n coverage for unwired modules | Localization review, 2026-09-18 | Not one of the five competitive gaps. New MVP screens in already-localized modules still ship their strings. |
| A centralized skill catalog and governance table | Review 15 | Free-text skills work for matching. |
| Nested route-segment `error.tsx` / `loading.tsx` below `app/app`, `app/portal` and `app/control` | Reviews 12–13 | The root boundaries and skeletons already exist. |
| CI build-once artifact; hosted-preview super-admin verification | Review 18 | CI speed and preview tooling, not product behavior. |
| Workflow integrations (Zapier-style) and native mobile apps | Competitive gap 5 and gap 2 scope | Explicitly post-MVP in the gap definitions. |
| Untested exports outside giving, finance, comms, child safety and pastoral | Review 18 (62 waivers) | The waiver list can only shrink (`test:surfaces`). MVP clears the risk-heavy modules (T1a, T1b), and the rest burns down after. |
| A team blockout calendar (every volunteer's unavailable days at a glance) | Review 20 | Admins see each volunteer's dates in the directory, and the planner already honors them. |
| Household blockouts (one entry for a whole family) | Review 20 | Needs household links in scheduling, which don't exist yet. |
| A self-service schedule page for staff roles (admin, pastor, leader, secretary) | Review 20 | `/app/member/schedule` is member-only. Staff can manage their own dates in the directory, except a secretary, who rarely volunteers. |
| Moving blocked dates when two profiles are merged | Review 20 | Merges are rare, and blockouts are future-only and quick to re-enter. |
| Rehearsal scheduling (G1.7, Story 4) | Owner decision 2026-09-29 | Cut to meet the Nov 6 date. Gap 1 closes on planning, assignment, notification and response; rehearsals are the next service-planning story after MVP. |
| A service plan requires its event (G1.8, Story 5) — backfill, NOT NULL, and keeping the event in sync with plan edits | Owner decision 2026-09-29, first half pulled forward 2026-09-30 | Cut to meet the Nov 6 date. G1.11 (2026-09-30) landed the create/link half: a new plan gets its own event at creation, an older plan gets one on its first assignment, so `volunteer_shifts.event_id` is already always set. ### 0.6 MVP release checklist (R2)

- [ ] Every Must row in §0.3 is `Done`, and every Stretch row is `Done` or `Deferred` with its reason.
- [ ] Each gap's definition of done is met, confirmed by the R1 whole-app Council, with no open Critical or High finding.
- [ ] Testing Council (v6) runs #1 (T3) and #2 (R1) are reported in `docs/reviews/`, with no open Critical or High defect.
- [ ] CI is green on `main`: `verify` (lint, typecheck, unit, `test:surfaces`, RLS audit, `test:db`) and all e2e shards, **as required checks** (O1).
- [ ] `npm run build` passes, `npm run lint:migrations` passes, and every migration applies cleanly to a fresh database.
- [ ] Safety track S1–S6 is done, and `RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md` passes.
- [ ] Every commit on `main` shows as verified.
- [ ] CHANGELOG release section, version bump and tag. README and this plan updated with the release and the re-baselined MVP score.

### 0.7 How this is tracked

- **On every merge:** the Documenter (Council step 7, in `.claude`, `.codex` and `.gemini`) sets the row's status to `Done (#PR)` and updates the progress line in §0.1. A PR for a tracked item names its ID in the title, e.g. `feat: blockout dates (G1.4)`.
- **On every milestone date:** compare against §0.2 and apply the cut line (§0.4). Record the result as a dated line under the milestone table.
- **New work:** beta feedback, Council findings and discovered bugs become rows (`B-n` for beta feedback, or the next ID in their gap or track). Anything not made a row goes into §0.5 with a reason. No open item lives only in chat or in a review document.
- **The history below (§ Current Status and History)** records what shipped and why. It is not where open work is tracked.

## 1. Project Vision & Scope

Build a secure, multi-tenant SaaS platform for churches called **ChurchCore**.

Product family context:
- **ChurchCore**: church operations platform (this plan and repository)
- **ChurchCore Care**: Christian counseling product
- **ChurchCore Academy**: Christian LMS and administration product

- Empower churches with tools for administration, donations, ministries, leadership, spiritual formation, events, and volunteer coordination.
- Key differentiators: role-based portals, user data ownership, strict PII and PHI handling, an intelligent categorized calendar, and AI-assisted tools with strong theological guardrails.
- Business model: subscription tiers with usage-based add-ons such as payments processing and AI credits.
- Architectural rule: the ChurchCore control plane and the tenant-facing church application are separate products with separate data boundaries.

## 2. User Roles & Portals (RBAC)

- **SuperAdmin**: Platform-wide management.
- **ChurchAdmin**: Full church settings, users, donations.
- **Secretary / Office Admin**: Daily Desk, calls, notes, visit scheduling, calendar coordination, and office follow-up without full church-admin access.
- **Pastor / Elder**: Leadership tools, sermon oversight.
- **MinistryAdmin / Leader**: Ministry tracking and volunteers.
- **Volunteer / Member**: Self-service portal for profile, giving, calendar, RSVPs, and ministries.

All pages and APIs enforce least-privilege RBAC.

Platform and tenant boundaries are also explicit:

- **Control Plane**: ChurchCore staff only
- **Tenant App**: Church users only

The two surfaces may share design systems and selected libraries, but they do not share a long-term runtime data model.

## 3. Core Features

- Member directory with families and attendance.
- Ministry membership and leadership assignment.
- Specialized ministry pathways for men, women, children, youth, young adults, marriage, education, missions, and outreach.
- Pastoral and minister profiles with fully customizable titles.
- Donation management with Stripe.
- Graphical multi-surface reporting and dashboards across members, events, giving, ministries, communications, and outreach.
- Communications across email and SMS.

## 4. AI Ministry Tools Suite

AI acts as an assistive tool only for research, brainstorming, and organization with strong theological guardrails. It never replaces prayer, Scripture study, or human discernment. All AI interactions are server-side, consent-aware, and audit logged.

- **Sermon Planning**: Idea and title brainstorming, outline generation, illustration suggestions, series planning, and calendar linkage.
- **Bible Study**: Conversational Q&A, passage analysis, personalized plans, small-group tools, and Scripture integration.
- **Prayer Journaling**: Guided entries, prompts, optional theme detection, answered prayer tracking, and Scripture links.
- **Daily Prayer Send-outs**: AI-assisted content with human approval, scheduling, and delivery through email, SMS, or in-app channels.
- **Weekly Bible Studies**: Auto-generated guides, discussion questions, leader and participant versions, and calendar integration.

Shared AI requirements:

- Prompt library with theological guardrails.
- Reliable Bible APIs using public-domain or licensed content.
- Retrieval and grounding for generated outputs.
- Disclaimers on all outputs.

Implementation starts in later sprints.

## 5. Events, Calendar & Volunteer Management

- Categorized calendar with categories such as General, Informational, Administrative, Ministry, Internal, Liturgical, Prayer, Outreach, Worship, and others as needed.
- FullCalendar with multiple views and powerful filters.
- RSVP system and volunteer shift management.
- Working-calendar support for Month, Week, Day, Agenda, and resource-aware views.
- Burnout guardrails including load warnings, rotation suggestions, and rest prompts.
- Specialized ministry stewardship metrics including vitality scoring, discipleship velocity, and children-serving safety monitoring.
- Real-time updates, conflict detection, and integrations with future AI support flows.

## 6. Technology Stack

- **Frontend**: Next.js 15 or newer with App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS, Mantine UI, and calendar tooling integrated into the Mantine-based application shell.
- **Backend/Database**:
  - Control plane backend and database for platform concerns
  - Tenant backend and database for church runtime concerns
  - Supabase remains acceptable for these layers, but the repo must no longer assume a single combined control-plane-plus-tenant database
- **Payments**: Stripe.
- **Notifications**: Twilio, SendGrid, or equivalent in later phases.
- **Hosting**: Vercel plus Supabase.
- **AI**: Private LLM endpoints in later sprints.

## 7. Security, Privacy & Compliance

- **PII and PHI Guidelines**: Classify member information, donations, pastoral notes, prayer journals, volunteer feedback, and any care-related records as sensitive. Enforce data minimization, encryption at rest and in transit, UI masking, and row-level security in the database.
- **High-Sensitivity Ministry Data**: Treat children's safety records, pickup data, marriage-care notes, pastoral review records, and mentorship access logs as higher-sensitivity slices requiring tighter RLS and field-level protection where needed.
- **User Data Ownership**: Provide self-service export and delete aligned to GDPR and CCPA-style expectations. Church admins may honor individual requests with logged overrides when permitted.
- **Consent & Auditing**: Require explicit consent for AI, communications, and tracking. Maintain full audit logs for sensitive access and role-sensitive actions.
- **AppSec**: Run SAST, SCA, DAST, dependency scanning, secrets scanning, and OWASP Top 10 verification on every PR. Require manual review for PII, payment, or AI changes.
- **Boundary Security**: Control-plane data and tenant operational data must live in separate databases. Cross-boundary support access must be explicit, auditable, and intentionally designed rather than implemented through shared tables.
- Non-production environments must use anonymized or safe development data. Regular penetration testing remains required before launch.

## 8. Sprint Roadmap

> **Historical.** This was the original sprint plan. Most of its scope shipped under different names through the Council process. Open work and dates are in [§0](#0-mvp-roadmap-to-november-6-2026-tracker).

| Sprint | Focus | Goal | Estimated Duration |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Foundation & Member Portal | Working auth, members, ministries, pastoral profiles, categorized calendar | 2 weeks |
| 2 | Admin Dashboard & Church Setup | Full admin tools, church settings, directory | 2 weeks |
| 3 | Events & Volunteer Management | Advanced calendar, RSVPs, volunteer tools | 2 weeks |
| 4 | Donations, Reporting & Financial Management | Stripe integration, dashboards, double-entry accounting, budgets, import | 3 weeks |
| 5 | AI Ministry Tools (Phase 1) | Sermon planner, Bible study assistant | 3 weeks |
| 6 | Communications & Polish | Notifications, mobile responsiveness | 2 weeks |
| 7+ | Advanced features, payment tiers, launch | Final polish and production launch | Ongoing |

## 9. Detailed Sprint 1 - Foundation & Member Portal

**Sprint Goal**: Deliver a functional core that churches can begin using immediately.

### 9.1 Sprint 1 Deliverables

- Working authentication on Supabase.
- Member portal foundation with church-scoped profile records.
- Ministry creation plus member-to-ministry assignment.
- Pastoral and minister profile titles as customizable display fields.
- Categorized calendar foundation inside the Mantine-based application shell.
- Baseline RLS across Sprint 1 tables.
- A documented migration path away from the current shared data-plane assumption and toward separate control-plane and tenant databases.

### 9.2 Database Schema (Supabase)

```sql
-- Churches (Tenants)
create table public.churches (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  name text not null,
  slug text unique not null,
  created_at timestamptz default now()
);

-- Profiles
create table public.profiles (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  user_id uuid references auth.users not null,
  church_id uuid references churches not null,
  full_name text not null,
  email text,
  phone text,
  address text,
  avatar_url text,
  role text check (role in ('church_admin', 'secretary', 'pastor_elder', 'ministry_leader', 'member_volunteer')) not null default 'member_volunteer',
  display_title text,
  is_pastoral boolean default false,
  created_at timestamptz default now()
);

-- Ministries
create table public.ministries (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  church_id uuid references churches not null,
  name text not null,
  description text,
  leader_profile_id uuid references profiles(id),
  created_at timestamptz default now()
);

-- Profile ↔ Ministry junction
create table public.profile_ministries (
  profile_id uuid references profiles(id) on delete cascade,
  ministry_id uuid references ministries(id) on delete cascade,
  primary key (profile_id, ministry_id)
);

-- Events
create table public.events (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  church_id uuid references churches not null,
  title text not null,
  description text,
  start timestamptz not null,
  "end" timestamptz not null,
  category text check (category in ('general', 'informational', 'administrative', 'ministry', 'internal', 'liturgical', 'prayer', 'outreach', 'worship')) not null default 'general',
  ministry_id uuid references ministries(id),
  visibility text check (visibility in ('public', 'members', 'leaders')) default 'members',
  rsvp_enabled boolean default true,
  created_by uuid references profiles(id),
  created_at timestamptz default now()
);

-- Enable RLS on all tables
alter table churches enable row level security;
alter table profiles enable row level security;
alter table ministries enable row level security;
alter table profile_ministries enable row level security;
alter table events enable row level security;
```

### 9.3 Sprint 1 Execution Order

1. Finish aligning the repo to the approved frontend direction for Sprint 1.
2. Freeze further architectural drift into the shared control-plane-plus-tenant database model.
3. Document and approve the control-plane versus tenant separation architecture.
4. Normalize the tenant schema around `churches`, `profiles`, `ministries`, `profile_ministries`, and `events`.
5. Wire church-scoped profile hydration after sign-in.
6. Build the member portal foundation on real tenant data.
7. Build ministry assignment flows.
8. Replace preview calendar state with categorized event records.

### 9.4 Sprint 1 Exit Criteria

- A signed-in church user lands in a real church-scoped app context.
- Profiles load from Supabase instead of preview role data.
- Ministries can be created and assigned within a church boundary.
- Events load with category support and basic calendar views.
- RLS blocks cross-church reads and writes.
- The repo no longer treats one shared control-plane-plus-tenant database as the target architecture.

## 10. SDLC & Development Processes

Agile with two-week sprints. Every code change follows:

1. Issue creation with bug or feature labels.
2. Design, including an ADR for major decisions.
3. Implementation on a feature branch.
4. Documentation covering what changed, why, PII impact, screenshots, testing notes, and ethical notes for AI or spiritual features.
5. Testing across unit, integration, manual, and security paths.
6. Review and verification.
7. Deployment through CI/CD.

**Versioning (SemVer)**:

- **MAJOR**: Breaking changes, major new modules, or significant PII or AI updates.
- **MINOR**: New features and enhancements.
- **PATCH**: Bug fixes and minor revisions.

Track delivery through GitHub Issues and Projects, `CHANGELOG.md`, and Releases. Major changes require stakeholder review.

## 11. GitHub Discipline & Code Quality

- **Branching**: GitHub Flow with `main` protected and feature or bugfix branches created from `main`.
- **PR Process**: Mandatory template including issue link, checklist for tests, docs, PII, security, ethical notes, and screenshots. Conventional commits. Minimum one to two approvals.
- **Checks**: ESLint and Prettier, tests, static analysis, dependency scanning, and secrets scanning.
- **Merge**: Squash and merge only after all checks and approvals pass.
- **Code Sanity**: Reviews focus on PR size limits, readability, performance, and security.
- **AppSec Verification**: CI-integrated with dedicated review for sensitive modules.

## 12. How to Use This Plan

1. Open this file before any work.
2. Reference relevant sections in every issue and PR.
3. Update this document only through PR when process or scope changes.
4. New contributors must read this first.
5. Treat it as the project constitution and keep it visible during active work.

**ADR 0002 Status** (completed April 25, 2026): ✅ DONE

The control-plane / tenant split is fully live:

- `lib/supabase/control-plane.ts` and `lib/supabase/tenant.ts` are separate, named clients.
- All data loaders use the correct surface client — no cross-boundary bleed.
- `supabase/control-plane/` contains the dedicated Supabase project (ref: `iopydttovnyjgikprvol`) config, schema, and seed data.
- All four control-plane env vars (`CONTROL_PLANE_SUPABASE_URL`, `CONTROL_PLANE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, `CONTROL_PLANE_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `CONTROL_PLANE_DB_URL`) are set in `.env.local` and in Vercel (Production + Preview).
- Tenant registry rows (`tenants`, `tenant_connections`) exist only in the control-plane project.
- The shared-project fallback has been removed from `lib/supabase/config.ts`.
- Migration `20260425010000_drop_control_plane_tables_from_tenant.sql` has been applied to the tenant project to drop the vestigial registry tables.

it also scoped the giving-statements e2e assertions to the Statements tab, because the hidden Analytics tab's rolling donor count also read "4 donors"). **O12 is done:** the owner verified churchcore.io in Resend, set `RESEND_API_KEY`, `RESEND_FROM_EMAIL` and `RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET` in Vercel, registered `https://www.churchcore.io/api/webhooks/resend` (the apex 308-redirects to www, so www is required) subscribed to `email.sent`, `email.delivered`, `email.delivery_delayed`, `email.bounced` and `email.complained` only (opens and clicks are excluded on purpose: consent §7, and the status-downgrade bug), and redeployed production; the orchestrator then ran `node --env-file=.env.local scripts/resend-unsent-receipts.mjs --apply`: 2 receipts sent through Resend, 0 failed, and the database confirms none are left unsent. **The Resend key in use is temporary; no PR yet, CI not run; no migration). `/app/communications/suppressions` lists every suppression with its reason in words (church admin, pastor and secretary can view); church admin only can add and remove. `removeSuppressionAction` lifts bounce and manual rows only, needs a reason of at least 5 characters, is church-scoped and audited; unsubscribe/STOP and spam-complaint rows are shown locked ("Only the person can opt back in", owner decision 2026-10-06). `suppressContactAction` now inserts or refuses a duplicate and returns `{ ok, error }` (it used to upsert, which let an admin overwrite a locked unsubscribe row with a removable "manual" one and then remove it, defeating the lock; found at build, confirmed by the Council). **Council fixes:** the table scrolls on phones (it was clipped at 390px), results are announced to screen readers, the silent 1,000-row cap now shows a notice, and the audit-failure message no longer claims the removal was unaudited (the table's trigger had already recorded the delete). **6 agent claims wrong or unsupported** (see the synthesis). **Recorded, not fixed:** two audit rows per removal (noisy, complete), no suppression retention policy, no "admins only" hint for pastors and secretaries, possible pre-hydration duplicate inputs on streamed pages. **Recounted from the tracker:** 33 Must rows, 23 merged (G5.1 as #188); [Superseded 2026-10-06: G5.1 merged as #188 (`12220b1`), O12 is done and Gap 5 is closed.] **Prior: MVP readiness 87/100 (Council Review 43, 2026-10-05, diff-scoped, five distinct agents under Council v2; synthesis status AMENDED, all three required fixes landed in `a961d21`), up from 86; it reaches 88 when owner action O12 confirms a live Resend send and Gap 5 closes.** G5.1 (Resend live, F2, ADR 0006) is built and Council Review 43-clean, **done pending merge** on `feat/resend-live-g5-1` (`93e883c` build, `a961d21` fixes; no PR yet, CI not run). One `selectEmailProvider()` routes both email paths (the communications queue and the direct `sendEmail()` used by receipts and notices) to Resend when `RESEND_API_KEY` and `RESEND_FROM_EMAIL` are set, else SendGrid, else not configured (production without keys still returns `provider_not_configured`). Resend requests follow its documented contract with an `Idempotency-Key`; both providers' HTTP statuses map to one shared set of codes (one shared transient constant); the provider is recorded on `communication_logs.provider`. A one-off `scripts/resend-unsent-receipts.mjs` (dry run by default, `--apply` to send, counts only) re-sends unsent donation receipts through the same claim/lease code (owner decision). No migration. **Found by the orchestrator from Resend's idempotency docs, all five seats missed it:** the first draft used a random key on the first send and `comm-log:<id>:attempt:<n>` on each retry, so a send that reached Resend but timed out on our side would be sent again under a new key. Fixed: the log row's id is created before the first send and `comm-log:<id>` is the key for the first send and every retry; other keys are prefixed by kind (`donation:`, `registration-payment:`); giving statements use `comm-log:<claimRowId>` (a fresh key per claim, an orchestrator change after the builder). Also fixed: `send-email.ts` is now `server-only`; the history page shows a plain-language failure reason and offers Retry only when the server-side `shouldRetryDelivery` says it can work. **Gap 5 does NOT close on merge.** The M3 line reads "Resend live", meaning email actually leaves production, so Gap 5 closes only after O12 confirms one real send (Agent 4 said it closes on merge; wrong). **New owner action O12** (see the tracker). **7 agent claims wrong or unsupported** (see the synthesis). **Recorded, not fixed:** no in-app provider-status indicator (S12 stays open); the Resend error-type field (`name` or `type`) is unverified (only the 409 `invalid_idempotent_request` distinction uses it); no live Resend call has been made; Resend's caching of failed responses for a reused key is undocumented (mitigated by per-claim statement keys). **Recounted from the tracker:** 33 Must rows, 22 merged (G3.3b merged as #184); remaining Must work, counting G5.1 until it merges (the standing convention): 11 rows (S11, G5.1, T1a, G2.1, G2.2, G4.1, G4.2, T1b, T2, R1, R2), 14.0 factory days, against 25 working days (Oct 5 - Nov 6), 11 days of slack. Next: S11, T1a for M3 (Fri Oct 23), plus O12. **Verified (orchestrator-run, after `a961d21`):** `npx vitest run` 206 files / 2,544 tests pass; `npm run lint` 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run test:surfaces` OK; builder: `npm run build` compiled, e2e `api-cron` + `api-webhooks` + `church-admin-readiness` 45 passed, the re-send script's dry run against local Supabase counted 2 and sent nothing. **Not verified: CI (`verify`, 4 `e2e` shards) has not run because no PR exists; GitHub's PR review has not run; no live Resend call has been made.** Details: `docs/factory-runs/2026-10-05-g5-1-resend-live.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-05-council-review-43-synthesis.md`.

[Superseded 2026-10-05: G3.3b merged as #184 (`3ec4bf7`), Gap 3 is closed.] **Prior: MVP readiness 86/100 (Council Review 42, 2026-10-05, diff-scoped, five distinct agents under Council v2; synthesis status AMENDED, both required fixes landed in `149af55`), up from 84: G3.3b (ChurchCore's own receipt for a paid event registration) is built and Council Review 42-clean, done pending merge on `feat/registration-receipt-g3-3b` (`18ed864` build, `149af55` fixes). No PR exists yet and CI has not run. Gap 3 closes on merge** against its own definition of done: G3.1-G3.3 and G3.3b are done, and the giving journeys in CI now include recurring "change" (the orchestrator found, while checking an agent's claim, that `tests/e2e/recurring-giving.spec.ts` never changed a gift; the step now exists). When a registration payment succeeds, the Stripe webhook sends one receipt (church, event and its church-local time, amount, "not a tax-deductible donation"), claimed with a 5-minute lease on `event_registration_payments` (migration `20261007000000`, additive, nullable `receipt_claimed_at`/`receipt_sent_at`) and marked sent only after the provider accepts; the previously unchecked payment update now checks its error. **Found by Agent 5 (Security), a pre-existing bug fixed this round:** `sendEmail()` returned `{ accepted: true }` in every environment when `SENDGRID_API_KEY`/`SENDGRID_FROM_EMAIL` were unset, so a keyless production deploy recorded donation receipts and failure notices as sent that never went out (the class Council Reviews 22 and 23 fixed in the communications adapters; this direct path was missed). It now returns `provider_not_configured` unless `stubsAllowed()`. The three callers (donation receipts, recurring failure notices, registration receipts) treat that as non-retryable: release the claim, log a warning, leave the sent marker unset (the receipt stays visibly unsent) and answer the webhook 200, so Stripe is not retried for days; every other refusal still throws and is retried. **Until an email provider is configured, receipts stay unsent by design: G5.1 must make receipts actually send in production.** **6 agent claims wrong or unsupported** (see the synthesis). **Recorded, not fixed:** a possible second (Stripe) receipt if a church enables Stripe receipts (same as donations), English-only receipts (S13), and unsent receipts not yet visible to operators (S12). **Owner actions:** O11 (apply migration `20261007000000` after merge); O8 and O10 done 2026-10-05; O9 key set, live `/hq` call still owed. **Recounted from the tracker:** 33 Must rows, 21 merged (G3.3b makes 22 on merge); remaining Must work, counting G3.3b until it merges (the standing convention): 12 rows (G3.3b, S11, G5.1, T1a, G2.1, G2.2, G4.1, G4.2, T1b, T2, R1, R2), 14.5 factory days, against 25 working days (Oct 5 - Nov 6), 10.5 days of slack. Next: S11, G5.1, T1a for M3 (Fri Oct 23). **Verified (orchestrator-run, after `149af55`):** `npx vitest run` 202 files / 2,459 tests pass; `npm run lint` 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run test:surfaces` OK; builder: `npm run build` succeeds, `npm run lint:migrations` PASS, migration applies to a fresh reset, `recurring-giving.spec.ts` + `api-webhooks.spec.ts` + `giving-statements.spec.ts` 31/31 pass locally (including the new "change" step), column-references DB test passes. **Not verified: CI (`verify`, 4 `e2e` shards) has not run because no PR exists; no real SendGrid or Stripe call has been made.** Details: `docs/factory-runs/2026-10-05-g3-3b-registration-receipt.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-05-council-review-42-synthesis.md`.

[Superseded: G3.3 merged as #181 (`b29a255`) on 2026-10-04 and G3.3b is now built (Council Review 42); "pending merge, no PR yet" and "Gap 3 is NOT closed until G3.3b lands" below were true when written.] **MVP readiness: 84/100** (Council Review 40, 2026-10-04, diff-scoped, five distinct agents under Council v2; synthesis status AMENDED, all required fixes landed in `55a2418`), up from 82: **G3.3 (year-end giving statements) is built and Council Review 40-clean, done pending merge on `feat/giving-statements-g3-3` (commits `60d912b` build, `55a2418` Council fixes). No PR exists yet and CI has not run.** An admin previews per-donor statements for a church-local date range (default: last calendar year), seeing who will be emailed and why anyone is skipped; downloads named-gift PDFs (`pdf-lib`, standard fonts); and sends an idempotent batch email whose body is the statement (claim-before-send under a partial unique index, migration `20261005000000`). A member downloads their own full statement, including their own anonymous gifts, from `/app/member/giving`. **Owner decision 2026-10-04 (after the factory validator's two High findings): anonymity holds against staff end to end.** Staff see named gifts only; anonymous gifts appear as one unattributed aggregate line; the admin PDF is named gifts only; the donor gets the full statement; statement send records carry no recipient. **Found by the orchestrator, not any Council seat (fixed):** the first build wrote `recipient_id` on statement send records, and the Communications history renders that by name to pastors and secretaries, exposing every anonymous-only donor and every named donor to roles with no giving access; now `recipient_id: null`. Also fixed: CR/LF stripped from the email subject (defence in depth), a 10-year range cap, a warning when a stale `sending` claim is released, and the rollback line in the migration. **7 agent claims wrong or unsupported against source**, including the Security seat's High "email header injection" (both providers take the subject as a JSON field, not an SMTP header). **Gap 3 is NOT closed until G3.3b lands** (new Must row, ~0.5 day, M3: ChurchCore's own receipt for a paid event registration, split out of G3.3 by owner decision 2026-10-04). M3 also needs S11, G5.1 and T1a. Owner action O8: apply migration `20261005000000` to the hosted database after merge. **Recounted from the tracker (2026-10-04):** 32 Must rows before this round (20 done; 12 open; the 4 deferred rows are not counted in the 32), now 33 with G3.3b. Remaining Must work, counting G3.3 until it merges (the standing convention): 13 rows (G3.3, G3.3b, S11, G5.1, T1a, G2.1, G2.2, G4.1, G4.2, T1b, T2, R1, R2), 16.5 factory days, against 25 working days (Oct 5 – Nov 6), about 8.5 days of slack (10.5 once G3.3 itself merges); slack is up from about 5.5 because G3.1 and G3.2 merged and stop counting, not because capacity grew. **Verified (orchestrator-run, after `55a2418`):** `npx vitest run` 199 files / 2,403 tests pass; `npm run lint` 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning in `localization-governance.config.mjs`); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run test:surfaces` OK; `npm run lint:migrations` PASS; migration `20261005000000` applies to a freshly reset local database (`npm run setup:e2e -- --reset`); `npm run test:e2e:local` on `giving-statements.spec.ts` and `api-session-routes.spec.ts`: 49 passed. **Not yet verified: CI (`verify`, 4 `e2e` shards) has not run, because no PR exists.** Details: `docs/factory-runs/2026-10-04-g3-3-giving-statements.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-10-04-council-review-40-synthesis.md`.

**MVP readiness holds at 82/100 (Council Review 39, 2026-10-03) — process infrastructure, no §0 row, no effect on the Nov 6 schedule.** This is the first round run under **Council v2**: a dedicated Security audit seat (Agent 5, alongside Data & API, Routes & Pages, UX & Shell, and Feature & Plan), the Documenter renumbered Agent 6, RATIFIED/AMENDED/REJECTED status labels on the synthesis (a recommendation; the owner decides), and a migration line added to the definition of done (`lint:migrations`, applies to a fresh reset DB, backwards-compatible with running code, a stated rollback) — applied consistently across `improve-software.md`, `AGENTS.md`, and the Claude, Codex and Gemini council skills. The same branch wired `/hq`'s AI advisor to a current model: `AI_HQ_MODEL` (default `claude-sonnet-5`) replaces the retired `claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022` default; the HQ register (open tasks, risks, decisions) loads server-side as the caller, PII-scrubbed (emails and ids only — the UI's prior "no PII is stored" claim was untrue and is now corrected), as model context; a new Council mode runs all five audit seats in parallel plus a synthesis, shown with a status badge and per-seat cards. A portable spec, `docs/council-and-hq-portable.md`, documents both pieces for reuse outside this repo. **Council Review 39** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-03-council-review-39-synthesis.md`, status AMENDED) found six fixable issues, all fixed the same round: a missing per-person rate limit on the six-model-call endpoint (3 Council runs / 10 advisor questions per minute, 429 beyond that); the caller, not the mode, chose the logged `hq_sessions.agent_id`/`agent_name`; `/control/launch-checklist` built its own nav with no HQ link; a Council result could land with no screen-reader announcement (now an `aria-live` `role="status"` region plus `scrollIntoView`, and a guard against double-submit); the "no PII is stored" copy, corrected above; and the Council test not asserting the response's shape. **7 agent claims wrong or unsupported against source** — a real own-sessions-only RLS policy (the deliberate S5 design) called a defect; `claude-sonnet-5` called an undocumented model id that "will 400" (an agent's model list predates it); the control-plane and tenant app assumed to share auth cookies (they're separate Supabase projects with separately named cookies); a superseded migration's `hq_*` RLS policies read instead of the current platform-admin-only ones (`20260930010000`); the in-app Council's limits said not to be shown in the UI (they are, in two places); hard-coded card gradients called an ADR 0026 violation (pre-existing, within the ratchet allowance, out of scope); and a claimed TOCTOU gap plus "6 sequential calls" (RLS re-checks on every read; the seat calls run in parallel) — see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory. An accepted risk is recorded: register free text reaches the model with no tool access and an advisory-only, text-rendered output (prompt injection has no path to action). **Verification:** `npx vitest run` (182 files / 2,146 tests) pass; `npm run test:surfaces` OK; `npm run lint` (0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated warning); `npm run build` compiled. No migration on this branch. **No PR exists yet for this branch, so the CI `e2e` job has not run — not independently confirmed; a draft PR is needed before this is treated as merge-ready.** **Also recorded this round: G3.1 and G3.2 (previous entry, below) merged to `main` as PR #177 (`5253915`) on 2026-10-03, CI green including all four `e2e` shards, and the hosted migration `20261004000000` was applied the same day — both are Done in §0's tracker.**

**MVP readiness: 82/100** (Council Review 38, 2026-10-02, diff-scoped, four distinct agents), up from 80: **G3.1 (recurring gifts) and G3.2 (installments from Stripe webhooks, retry-safe webhook) merged to `main` as PR #177 (`5253915`) on 2026-10-03, shipped together on one branch (owner decision) so no card is ever charged without being recorded.** `recurring_gifts` (migration `20261004000000`) holds a member's plan, backed by a Stripe subscription on the church's own connected account (ADR 0025) — members read their own, managers their church's, only the server writes. Starting today charges the first installment in the card step; a future start (up to a year ahead) saves the card via a SetupIntent and trials to a charge at the church's own midnight on its start date. Members set up, change (no proration), pause, resume and cancel from `/app/member/giving`; church admins see a Recurring tab, never naming an anonymous giver. The old `cancelRecurringDonationAction`, which worked on `donations` rows nothing had ever created, is removed. `invoice.paid` records each installment as a donation keyed by its invoice and completes it through a shared `completeDonation` (succeeded, then the repeatable ledger post, then the claimed-before-sending receipt, then `completed_at`, written last); `invoice.payment_failed` records the failure and marks the gift past due; `customer.subscription.updated`/`deleted` sync the gift, ordered by event time. The webhook route now answers 5xx on a handler failure, so Stripe retries, instead of 200 — G3.2's widened definition of done (Council Review 31). `/give/[slug]` now takes real one-time gifts on the church's own account in production, Frequency removed — closing G3.1's widened scope (Council Review 37): it had been a mock-up reachable only in demo mode. **Found by the orchestrator (fixed):** the mock public giving page; `cancelStripeSubscription` called `POST /v1/subscriptions/:id/cancel`, an endpoint that doesn't exist for subscriptions (Stripe cancels one with `DELETE /v1/subscriptions/:id`), so no recurring gift could ever have been cancelled at Stripe — pre-existing, the same class as Council Review 34's malformed PaymentIntent parameter; the webhook answering 200 on handler errors. **Fixed after Council:** a subscription created before a later failure is now cancelled at Stripe too; a gift whose card was never confirmed stays incomplete instead of acting like a real gift; a retry e2e proving a signed `invoice.paid` resumes a half-finished installment with no second receipt and a repeated delivery records nothing twice; the admin tab label is in i18n; the start date states its time zone; focus moves to the card step; the receipt copy is accurate. **6 agent claims wrong or unsupported against source** — a "Critical: missing role check" (the actions are scoped to the caller's own profile, like the one-time gift actions); "the manifest should mark actions public" (no such field exists); "teal violates the design system" (ADR 0026 keeps teal as the secondary accent); "demo card numbers above the real Stripe form" (no Stripe form renders in demo mode); "no warning for a member with no email" (starting a gift requires one); and Agent 4 inverting the actual flow ("the SetupIntent is confirmed before the subscription exists," when it's the reverse) — see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory. **Owner decisions:** updating the card on a recurring gift is new Should row **S20** (~1 day); the cron cancelling an incomplete gift's subscription after 24 hours is new Should row **S21** (~0.5 day); an admin creating a recurring gift on someone's behalf is a new post-MVP row; **O7 widens** to three more Connect webhook events (`invoice.paid`, `invoice.payment_failed`, `customer.subscription.updated`) and a real test-mode run covering a future-dated gift and a paid installment. **This close-out also found and corrected two stale statuses: D1 and G3.0c had each already merged (PR #176, PR #175) before this round started, but their rows still read "pending merge,"** and fixed G3.2's Est. column (2 → 2.5, the Council Review 31 widening had only ever been stated in prose). Corrected: **remaining Must work is now 14 rows, 20.5 factory days, against 26 working days (Oct 2 – Nov 6), about 5.5 days of slack** — up from about 4, mostly from the status correction, since G3.1 and G3.2 themselves still count until merged. **Verified (re-run by the Documenter):** `tsc` clean; `npm run lint` exit 0; `npm run test:surfaces` OK; `npx vitest run` (**2,209 tests / 185 files**); `npm run test:db` (**91 tests**, including the new `tests/database/recurring-gifts-rls.test.ts`); `npm run lint:migrations` PASS; `npm run build` compiled. Full local e2e: 816 passed, 0 failed (11 known local "denied" timeouts, all passing on retry). `tests/e2e/recurring-giving.spec.ts` with `api-webhooks`: 25/25. Commit signatures verified. One new migration, `20261004000000_recurring_gifts.sql`. **Update (Documenter, Council Review 39 close-out, 2026-10-03): merged to `main` as PR #177 (`5253915`), CI green including all four `e2e` shards; the migration was applied to the hosted database the same day.** Volunteer Scheduling unchanged at ~90%. Not more, because G3.3 (statements) is open and nothing has run against real Stripe yet (O7).

**Prior: MVP readiness 80/100** (Council Review 36, 2026-10-02, diff-scoped, four distinct agents), up from 79: **G3.0c (card form for paid event registrations) is built and Council Review 36-clean, pending PR.** A paid registration from the public portal or a member now gets Stripe's card form on the church's own connected account (ADR 0025), right after registering, replacing the "Secure payment ready" panel, which had no way to pay — a paid registration is refused before anything is written when the church can't take payments, Stripe errors are no longer swallowed, and leaving the payment step cancels the PaymentIntent then the registration. **The admin "add registrant" path doesn't refuse (owner-confirmed):** it now records the payment as due and tells the admin to collect it in person, instead of creating a PaymentIntent nobody could ever pay — an admin can always add a walk-in, even at an unconnected church. **Found by the orchestrator while building, fixed:** `registerForEventAction` had no session or role check (RLS was its only gate); it now has the event page's own gate. **Council Review 36** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-02-council-review-36-synthesis.md`) confirmed G3.0c meets its row, then fixed, owner-approved, in `50dc819`: an up-front cancel warning, the card step's "Cancel registration" label, a heading that takes focus, and cancelling an orphaned PaymentIntent when the payment row fails. **6 agent claims wrong or unsupported against source**, including Agent 4's third unevidenced competitor matrix in a row (Reviews 29, 35, 36) — see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory. **Owner decisions:** the admin-path behavior confirmed as built; new Should row **S17** (resume an unpaid registration from its confirmation email); ChurchCore's own registration receipts join **G3.3**; the new payment strings join **S13**. **This close-out also found and corrected a stale status: G3.0b had already merged as PR #174 before this round, but its row and the §0.1 capacity paragraph still read "pending merge."** Correcting that drops G3.0b out of "remaining Must work" for the first time since Review 35 computed it: **remaining Must work is now 15 rows, 21 factory days, against 26 working days (Oct 2 – Nov 6), about 5 days of slack** (not the 16 rows/24 days/~2 days the uncorrected figure would have shown; G3.0c itself still counts, pending its own merge). **Verified (re-run by the Documenter):** `tsc`; `npm run lint` (0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated warning); `npm run test:surfaces` OK; `npx vitest run` (**2,113 tests / 179 files**); `npm run test:db` (**86 tests**); `npm run build` compiled.
