- **The signed-in person's church profile id — S7; Council Review 21 (`fix/session-church-profile-id`, draft PR #158, 2026-09-28):** found by G1.5's own e2e journey, not by a Council round — communication-log writes were failing `communication_logs_sent_by_fkey`, and the cause turned out to be app-wide. `session.profile.id` is the **auth (login) user id**. It has never been a church `profiles.id`: since migration `20260420000000`, `handle_new_user()` (and `provision-tenant.mjs`) create profiles with `gen_random_uuid()`, so **every real user created since April 20, 2026 was affected**, not just seed data.
  - **What was broken.** 51 places wrote the login id into a column that references `profiles`. Members' own My Schedule was always empty; in-app confirm/decline and signed-in blockout dates failed; communication-log writes silently violated their foreign key and were lost. Admin create flows across service planning, songs, reminders, hours, communication templates/suppressions, finance journals/budgets, GL posting, group meetings/attendance, operations documents/onboarding, and shepherd workflows all violated their `profiles` foreign keys. The `ai_interactions` AI audit trail was never written. Erasure and localization governance were unusable (see corrections below). The local-SQL sandbox hydrate's `id != <login id>` deleted every profile in the church, including the admin's own — S7 makes it `id != NULL`, which deletes nothing.
  - **The fix.** The session gains `session.churchProfileId` (the person's `profiles.id` in the church in context; null when they have none there, e.g. a platform admin viewing a tenant). Each of the 51 usages was classified by the column it actually feeds; the 7 kept on the login id are correct, because `audit_log.actor_id` holds `auth.uid()`, not a profile id. "Act as myself" loaders degrade to empty instead of erroring; actions refuse clearly (`lib/church-profile-id.ts`'s `requireChurchProfileId`) instead of writing a bad id. `respondToShiftAction` now updates through a scoped admin client (ADR 0022's pattern), because members have no UPDATE policy on `volunteer_shifts` and the direct update was silently matching zero rows while reporting success.
  - **Council Review 21** (`docs/reviews/2026-09-28-council-review-21-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, 76 files): the core fix confirmed correct by Agents 1 and 2 (all 51 switched usages land in nullable `profiles`-referencing columns; none reaches an `auth.users` column; no null reaches a NOT NULL column). Six findings, all landed on this branch as P1–P6 (owner-approved):
    - **P1 erasure.** `lib/actions/erasure.ts:36` was misclassified in S7 itself as an audit-actor usage and kept on the login id — but the same variable drives the self-erasure guard and the `erase_profile_pii` actor lookup, so erasure always failed with "Only church admins may erase profile PII," and the self-erase guard could never fire. Now uses `churchProfileId`, refusing an admin with no church profile before any database call.
    - **P2 localization governance.** `assigned_by` and the reviewer identity used the login id against a NOT NULL `profiles`-referencing column and a profile-id comparison — every assignment violated its foreign key, and every review submission failed `reviewer_not_assigned`. Fixed; the audit actor stays the login id.
    - **P3 data export.** Memberships were read by `church_memberships.profile_id`, a column that doesn't exist; now reads by `user_id = session.userId`.
    - **P4 no throws to the UI.** `joinGroupAction` reached the error page (its caller had no catch); elders AI mislabelled a missing profile as "AI temporarily unavailable." Both, plus `respondToShiftAction`, now return `{ ok: false, code }` instead of throwing, translated in en/es/es-PR; shift responses are accepted only for shifts that haven't happened yet. Donation and data-rights throws are explicitly deferred to S8, which rewrites those writes anyway; shepherd keeps its caught throw (admin-only).
    - **P5 member schedule.** No more "Invalid Date" when a shift has no plan (falls back to the shift's own date); es-PR dates render correctly; the time range is shown; each card has its own pending state; a translated notice appears for a viewer with no church profile.
    - **P6 tests that tell the ids apart.** Agent 2's finding that "the 115 mechanically updated fixtures prove nothing" (they set `churchProfileId === profile.id`, so they'd pass with either id) was accepted. **109 fixtures now use a login id distinct from the church profile id**; the whole suite still passes, and only two audit-actor assertions needed to change (they had been asserting the wrong id — audit actors are login ids). `queue-communication` now asserts `sent_by` is the church profile id, and the cron/retry synthetic sessions set `churchProfileId: null` explicitly.
  - **Still broken, tracked as new rows, not fixed here:** member writes RLS doesn't allow — giving (`donations` insert/update are management-only, and `initiateDonationAction` creates a Stripe PaymentIntent *before* the failing insert), mobile check-in (no member `attendance` insert), group join (no member `group_members` insert), RSVP (the `event_rsvps` policy compares a profile id with `auth.uid()`), registration capacity (an RLS-filtered count) and payments (an admin-only upsert) — **S8**. A pre-existing, unrelated privilege escalation into `/hq`: members can update their own `profiles.role`, `church_id`, `user_id` and `membership_status` (`profiles_update_self` plus column grants), and `current_user_role()`, which gates `/hq` and every `hq_*` policy, trusts `profiles.role` — **S5**, moved to Week 2 and widened.
  - **Score re-base.** Reviews 9–20 counted these modules as working because the e2e sweep only ever proved pages render — it never exercised a write as a non-platform user. Agent 4 re-bases the true Review 20 figure to **about 64/100** (Volunteer Scheduling ~70%, Giving ~45%, member-portal writes ~40%), then **67/100** once S7 merges (+3 for the restored create flows and member self-service). Returning to 71 or more needs S8.
  - **Corrections made during this round, not accepted as first written** (`feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory, two new instances): **(a)** S7's own classification of `erasure.ts:36` was wrong — see P1 above, our own commit's classification, not an agent's claim, was the error this time. **(b)** a whole class of evidence — the 115 mechanically updated test fixtures — proved nothing until P6 made 109 of them use distinct ids; Agent 2's "these tests prove nothing" claim was verified and accepted, not just asserted. **(c)** our own prior write-up (Council Review 20's history bullet, and the G1.4 CHANGELOG entry) claiming signed-in blockout dates "worked" was wrong: only the volunteer-link path had ever been exercised end to end; the signed-in path was untested and broken until this branch.
  - **Tracker:** new rows **S8** (Must, 2 days, member writes vs. RLS, must land before G1.5 resumes and before G3.1) and **S9** (Must, 0.5 day, rename `profile.id` to read as the login id, a lint rule, one id resolver, 0-row checks on member updates) added to Week 1. **S5** moved to Week 2 and widened with the `profiles` column lock, `current_user_role()` reading `church_memberships`, and dropping the `profiles.role` fallback in `lib/auth.ts`. **T3** gains a lens for a non-platform user creating through the UI. **G3.1** now depends on S8. Capacity: 36 Must days against 25 available days, about 44% over — the owner decides date vs. scope at M1 (Oct 2).
  - No new ADR — `churchProfileId` makes an existing distinction explicit (`lib/church-profile.ts` already had `resolveActiveChurchProfileId`, underused); the admin-client-with-server-side-scoping pattern for member writes is ADR 0022's, confirmed independently in synthesis.
  - Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run lint` (0 errors, 1 pre-existing warning); `npx vitest run` (**1,807/1,807 passed**, 154 files); `npm run test:db` (**34 passed**); `npm run test:surfaces` (also removed a now-stale `generateDataExportAction` waiver, since P3 tests it); `npm run lint:migrations` (pass); `npm run test:e2e:local` (**124 passed**), covering the member self-service journeys (schedule and confirm, blocked dates), the rotation, link and notification-free journeys, and the member/volunteers/groups/localization/data-rights pages × every role.
  - Full agent reports: [agent-1-database-api](docs/reviews/2026-09-28-council-review-21-agent-1-database-api.md), [agent-2-route-page](docs/reviews/2026-09-28-council-review-21-agent-2-route-page.md), [agent-3-ux-shell](docs/reviews/2026-09-28-council-review-21-agent-3-ux-shell.md), [agent-4-feature-competitive](docs/reviews/2026-09-28-council-review-21-agent-4-feature-competitive.md).
  - **Documenter note (this close-out):** the P1–P6 fix commit is on the local branch but **not yet pushed** to `origin/fix/session-church-profile-id` / PR #158 — the PR's current head is `14461db` (S7 alone). CI on the PR reflects the pre-P1–P6 commit only. Push the fix commit and get a fresh green CI run, including the `e2e` job, before merging.

- **Member writes vs. RLS — S8; Council Review 22 (`fix/member-writes-rls-s8`, 2026-09-29, on top of S7/`29d1776`):** S7 fixed which id a signed-in write uses; S8 fixes that members had almost no RLS grant to write with at all. Found by Council Review 21: giving, mobile check-in, group join and event registration all had no member INSERT/UPDATE policy, and the `event_rsvps` self policy compared a profile id with `auth.uid()`.
  - **The fix (`4377e49`).** Writes that carry business rules — donations, attendance, `group_members`, registration capacity, payments — run server-side through a church-scoped admin client after the action's own checks (ADR 0022's pattern), not new member RLS policies, so a member can't skip those rules by writing the table directly. A member-JWT DB harness (`tests/database/member-writes-rls.test.ts`) proves the direct writes stay denied. `event_rsvps` gets the one RLS fix, migration `20260929000000`: own profile, own church, an RSVP-enabled event only. Journeys for giving, group join, RSVP and full-event registration in `tests/e2e/member-writes.spec.ts`.
  - **Found while fixing.** Donation confirmation trusted the client instead of requiring Stripe to report the PaymentIntent `succeeded`. Members had no SELECT on `event_registration_settings` (it holds access codes) and PostgREST's object-shaped `events` embed was read as `[0]`, so member registration options were always empty. Data-rights and donation actions now return errors instead of throwing, with 0-row checks.
  - **Council Review 22** (`docs/reviews/2026-09-29-council-review-22-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, 19 files, conditional GO from Agent 1): scoping confirmed correct everywhere checked (church id and profile id always come from the session, never the client). Six findings, landed as P1–P8 in a follow-up commit (`7ea5504`), owner-approved:
    - **P1 stub mode.** `lib/stripe/donations.ts`'s stub returned `"succeeded"` with no `NODE_ENV`/demo-mode check — on any deploy without Stripe keys, a member could record unlimited "succeeded" gifts with emailed tax receipts. Now runs only outside production, or with `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO_MODE=true`.
    - **P2 no orphans while G3.0 is unbuilt.** With Stripe configured but no card form to pay a created PaymentIntent, `initiateDonationAction` now returns "isn't available yet" before writing a row or calling Stripe; the giving page shows the notice up front, computed server-side, and disables Give.
    - **P3 confirm posts to the GL.** `autoPostToGlSupabase` moved out of the webhook route into a shared `server-only` `lib/stripe/donation-completion.ts`, called from both the webhook and `confirmDonationAction` — whichever wins the `pending → succeeded` update now posts and receipts. Before this, a gift confirmed by the browser instead of the webhook never reached the ledger.
    - **P4 registration visibility and household insert.** The settings read had bypassed `events.visibility`, so a member with an event id could register for a staff-only event — now enforced again on the Supabase path. The registration insert (and household-profile reads) moved to the admin client so a household member's row can be read back.
    - **P5 no raw database text to members.** Check-in and registration errors log the raw message and return plain text instead. Mobile check-in gets Supabase-path unit tests (it had none).
    - **P6 escaped receipts.** `donor_name` and `fund_designation`, both client-supplied, are HTML-escaped in the confirm and webhook receipts — previously exploitable to any address once P1's stub was reachable.
    - **P7 the two member writes S8's own audit missed.** A re-submitted pending change request updated 0 rows and silently dropped the new changes (`member_change_requests` has no member UPDATE policy) — now goes through the admin client, scoped to church, request id and target profile, with a row-count check. A second save of notification preferences hit a unique-key error — the upsert now targets `onConflict: "church_id,profile_id"`.
    - **P8 manifest.** `member-writes.spec.ts` registered under `/app/[role]`; one waiver retired.
  - **Not fixed here, tracked as tracker changes:** **S10** (new Must row) — the public event registration page's settings read still returns nothing even after the `[0]` fix, because visitors also have no SELECT on `event_registration_settings`; and `event_registrations_public_insert with check (true)` lets anyone with the anon key insert a confirmed, paid registration for any church, pre-existing and unrelated to S8. **S5** widened to lock the data-rights approval columns, `is_pastoral` and `safety_clearance_date` against self-edits. **S9** widened to add a church filter to three self policies that currently pick "a" profile by `user_id` alone. **G3.0**'s DoD gains removing S8's giving guard and cancelling an abandoned gift's PaymentIntent. Four minor items (an already-waived throwing action, a payment-upsert bookkeeping log, a lost UI-only "Requested" state on reload, the non-atomic capacity count) recorded in §0.5 with reasons, not fixed.
  - **Corrections, not accepted as first written.** Our own S8 commit message and `DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md` note overstated two things: it implied the public registration page now works (the `[0]` fix was necessary but not sufficient — see S10, above) and it claimed "every member-reachable write is audited," which Agent 4 disproved with the change-request finding (P7). No agent claim was found wrong this round — a first, after four consecutive rounds (16, 17, 20, 21) with an error somewhere in our own write-up. Agent 4's "inferred" preferences-upsert finding was independently verified by reading alone, not just accepted narratively.

- **Assignment notifications — G1.5; Council Review 23** (`feat/assignment-notifications-g1-5`, commits `83b7a2d` build, `a99cfea` manifest, `34535c1` fixes, on top of S7/S8): G1.5 was built 2026-09-28 and paused when its own e2e journey found S7 (the login id used as a profile id); after S7 and S8 merged it was rebased cleanly and the journey passed locally (assign → the volunteer gets a link → declines → the admin finds a replacement). This branch also carries `2d71473`, the docs-only record of the owner's 2026-09-29 date/scope decision (MVP → Nov 6), out of this round's scope.
  - **The fix (`83b7a2d`/`a99cfea`).** Assigning a volunteer, by hand or by auto-fill, gives that shift its own confirmation token (unique, 128-bit, valid to the service date + 7 days, reused and only extended while unexpired — replacing the old fixed-14-day, manual-reminder-only token from Council Review 20) and sends an email or SMS with the accept/decline link, through the existing `sendWithSuppression` → `queueCommunicationAction` pipeline (consent and suppression respected, ids correct per S7/S9). A decline surfaces ranked replacement suggestions via "Find replacement." The roster shows the reason when an assigned volunteer has since blocked the date. A failed or skipped notification never undoes the assignment.
  - **Council Review 23** (`docs/reviews/2026-09-29-council-review-23-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, 15 files, +900/−94): all four agents confirm the core is correct — no new `"use server"` exports, the three widened actions keep their write gate, ids are church profile ids, a failed notification never undoes the assignment, 7 of 8 G1.5 DoD clauses done. Eight findings, landed as P1–P7 in a follow-up commit (`34535c1`), owner-approved:
    - **P1 (Blocking) — every member could read every volunteer's confirm token.** `volunteer_shifts_select_member_scope` is `belongs_to_church(church_id)`, and `authenticated` held table-wide SELECT including `confirmation_token` — a member could fetch another volunteer's token through PostgREST and decline their shift or read their schedule via the public token pages. Pre-existing, but G1.5 put a token on every shift (0 of 7 local shifts had one before), turning a latent exposure into a real one. Fixed: migration `20260929010000` revokes table-wide SELECT and grants `authenticated` SELECT on every column except `confirmation_token` (the migration notes a new column needs its own grant); the two staff-side token reads (Remind, `notifyVolunteerOfShift`) and `ensureShiftToken`'s update move to the church-scoped admin client (ADR 0022's pattern) with a row-count check; `lib/volunteer-data.ts` selects explicit columns instead of `select("*")`. DB test in `tests/database/volunteer-shift-token.test.ts`: a member can't read the token; everything else they could read, they still can.
    - **P2 — SMS-preferring volunteers were never notified.** `checkOptIn` treats a missing `notification_preferences` row as SMS off and `sms_opt_in` defaults to false, so `chooseChannel`'s SMS choice was always skipped, with no fallback — all 5 local SMS-preferring profiles were affected. Fixed: falls back to email when SMS is preferred but consent is off or there's no phone, and the volunteer has email with email consent; the outcome tells the admin why ("Emailed instead (they haven't opted in to texts)").
    - **P3 — no faked deliveries.** The SendGrid, Twilio and Resend stubs (`accepted: true` with a `*-stub-*` id whenever keys are unset, in any environment) now run only outside production or in demo mode — the same `lib/stub-mode.ts` rule as Review 22's Stripe stub. In production without keys, the adapter returns `provider_not_configured` (non-transient) and the admin sees "email isn't set up for this church yet" instead of "Email sent."
    - **P4 — links could point at a dead address.** `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL ?? "http://localhost:3000"` disagreed with the rest of the app's `:4200` fallback, and `??` let an empty string produce a relative link. Fixed: one resolver, `lib/app-url.ts` — `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` (trimmed, non-empty) if set, else `http://localhost:4200` outside production, else `null` in production, so a production deploy without the variable skips the send instead of mailing a dead `localhost` link.
    - **P5 — messages and admin feedback.** The church name is now in the subject and first line (a message with no church name read as spam); SMS is trimmed to about two segments; a notification that wasn't sent now shows a yellow toast instead of the always-green one (the status shown even for a skip or failure); the confirm page's stale "expire after 14 days" copy now matches the shift-date-+7 rule; "Find replacement" now also shows for a volunteer who blocked the date without declining; the person who just declined is excluded from that date's replacement suggestions (the same-day check and `get_volunteer_pool` had ignored declined shifts, so the decliner could rank top for their own vacated slot).
    - **P6 — Remind could send twice.** The send happened before the `volunteer_shift_reminders` insert, so an insert failure returned `ok: false` after the email had already gone out, and the reminder counter incremented on a skipped or failed send. Fixed: the reminder is recorded only when actually sent; a post-send record failure returns success with a warning, never a false `ok: false`.
    - **P7 — tests and docs.** Unit fixtures gain distinct login/profile ids with `sent_by` asserted (S7's rule — the prior fixtures couldn't have caught a regression); the e2e now checks `confirmation_status = 'declined'` in the DB, not just UI text, and that the decliner isn't suggested back; the list-page manifest entry (which had `volunteer-schedule.test.tsx`, though that test renders only the detail page) is corrected; Gap 1's DoD drops "rehearsal" (see the correction below); this tracker row's status.
  - **Corrections.** Gap 1's definition of done (§0.3) still listed "rehearsal" among the CI journeys though the owner moved G1.7 after MVP earlier the same day (2026-09-29) — our own doc edit from the date/scope decision missed it; fixed by P7. No agent claim was found wrong this round.
  - **Not fixed here, recorded in §0.5:** decline alerts to the admin who made the assignment (a Stretch candidate, positioned after G1.10 in the cut order if promoted); volunteer message language (joins the existing localization deferral); `applyPlanAutoFillAction` sending sequentially (fine at MVP scale).
  - **Score.** **70/100** (Agent 4, accepted), up from 69: service-planning notifications work end to end. Not more, because deliveries still depend on stubbed or unwired providers (G5.1, F2) and G1.6 is open. Volunteer Scheduling ~86%.

- **Church-timezone days — G1.6; Council Review 24** (`feat/church-timezone-days-g1-6`, commits `ad09cab` build, `2cca5bf` fixes, on top of G1.5/`438fa32`): `volunteer_shifts.starts_at`/`ends_at` are `timestamptz`, but every live writer (`shiftWindowForPlan`, the seed, the e2e fixtures) stores the church's local wall-clock time with no offset, so a shift's stored UTC date is already its own local day — same-day conflicts, the pool's `serving_on_date`, the 30-day load, the month count and blocked dates were already correct. What was wrong was every comparison against **"today" or "now"**, computed in UTC: at a UTC−4 church, from 8 pm local a member couldn't answer for or mark off that evening's service, and it dropped off their schedule hours before it started.
  - **The fix (`ad09cab`).** New `lib/church-time.ts`: `todayInTimeZone()` (the church's today) and `startOfDayInTimeZone()` (the real instant a local day begins, DST-safe). Applied to shift responses, blockout add/remove/list (member, token link, admin), the member schedule, the upcoming/past service-plan split, and confirm-link expiry. New [ADR 0023](docs/adr/0023-volunteer-shift-wall-clock-times.md) records the wall-clock convention: keep it rather than migrate every shift to a true instant (a data migration outside G1.6's half-day estimate and the MVP's zero buffer), and how to compare a shift time (wall-clock) against a real instant (`now()`, `confirmation_token_expires_at`).
  - **Council Review 24** (`docs/reviews/2026-09-29-council-review-24-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, 11 files): ADR 0023's central claim confirmed by Agents 1 and 4 (every live writer is wall-clock; the local data and shift-to-shift day math bear it out) and every changed signature's callers pass the church's zone (Agent 2). Seven findings landed as P1–P7 in a follow-up commit (`2cca5bf`), owner-approved:
    - **P1 — the emailed-link schedule still compared with the real `now`.** `getPublicVolunteerScheduleByToken` had the same UTC bug G1.6 fixed in `getMemberSchedule`, left on the page most volunteers actually use — at a UTC−4 church a 10 am shift vanished at 6 am local and an 8 pm service at 4 pm. Now lists from the church's today, with a regression test.
    - **P2 — the admin plan list re-split upcoming/past in UTC on the client**, overriding the server's church-day list — after 8 pm at a UTC−4 church, tonight's plan moved to "Past." The schedules page now passes the church's today down to the component, with a component test at an evening clock.
    - **P3 — `church-time` correctness.** `startOfDayInTimeZone` was an hour early on the day a DST transition skips midnight (Santiago, Havana) — the two-pass fix-up oscillated and landed on the prior day's 23:00, which also made `tokenExpiryFor` throw on a malformed day instead of falling back. Fixed for both, with tests for Santiago, Havana and Kolkata.
    - **P4 — the convention wasn't enforced where it can break.** `assignVolunteerAction` trusted the client's `startsAt`/`endsAt` as sent (an offset-bearing value would have been stored as a real instant) — now derives the shift window on the server and refuses an offset. The church settings action accepted any string as a time zone (an unknown zone silently became UTC) — now accepts only a valid IANA zone.
    - **P5 — tests for the two loaders G1.6 itself fixed but never tested**, `getServicePlanList` and `getMemberSchedule`, at an evening UTC−4 clock.
    - **P6 — small fixes.** Run-of-service item times (stored the same wall-clock way as shifts) now display in UTC instead of the browser's zone, per ADR 0023; answering from the emailed link now refuses a shift that's already happened, with a row-count check, like the signed-in path; an import-statement placement fix.
    - **P7 — docs.** ADR 0023 corrected: it had listed the member schedule and the plan split as already fixed when the token schedule and the client split weren't, and it misdescribed burnout as counting from `now()` (it counts 30 days back from the target shift — wall-clock vs. wall-clock, exact only on a UTC server clock, which Vercel and CI are); `get_volunteer_pool`'s last-served (`starts_at <= now()`) added to the accepted small-offset exceptions; the DB session-TimeZone dependency noted. The G1.6 DoD gains a one-line amendment (§0.3): the conflict, load and month clauses were already met by the convention; only today/now needed converting.
  - **Correction, our own write-up (sixth consecutive round, see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory):** ADR 0023's first draft overstated twice — it listed the member schedule and the plan split as fixed while the emailed-link schedule and the client-side split still used UTC, and it misdescribed burnout. Both caught by Council Review 24, not by this branch's own testing. **No agent claim was found wrong this round.**

  - **Score.** **71/100** (Agent 4, accepted), up from 70. Volunteer Scheduling ~88%. Not higher: deliveries still depend on G5.1, and Gap 1's plan-build/songs journey is open.

- **Service-plan build journey — G1.11; Council Review 25** (`test/service-plan-build-journey-g1-11`, commits `1233705` build, `b867174` Council Review 25 fixes, on top of G1.6/`94f49cf`): a new e2e journey (`tests/e2e/service-plan-build.spec.ts`) creates a service plan, adds a song that isn't in the library (creating it) and again from the library, adds a position, assigns a volunteer by hand and auto-fills, checking the database at every step — closing Gap 1's CI-journey clause that Council Review 24 found unmet (`service-plan-rotation.spec.ts` only ever used a seeded plan). It found two real bugs, both fixed in `1233705`:
  - **A plan saved without a linked event could never be staffed** — every assignment failed on `volunteer_shifts.event_id`'s NOT NULL with a raw database error. A new plan now gets its own event, at its date and time in the church's zone (`zonedTimeToInstant`); an older plan without one gets it on its first assignment. **Owner decision 2026-09-30:** this pulls forward the first half of G1.8 (a service plan requires its event); the backfill migration and the `NOT NULL` constraint stay deferred (§0.5).
  - **Adding a position didn't raise the plan's Unfilled count**, so the count was wrong and Auto-fill stayed hidden until a reload.
  - **Council Review 25** (`docs/reviews/2026-09-30-council-review-25-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped; Agents 2 and 3 combined into one report for a change this size; Agent 1 briefly created and deleted a read-only-rule-breaking probe file in `lib/`, confirmed cleaned up): the two fixes confirmed correct by all three agents (RLS matches the write gate for every role, `created_by` is the church profile id, the race guard is sound), but found the fix itself opened four gaps, landed as P1–P5 in `b867174`, owner-approved:
    - **P1 — staff-only auto-events.** The new event defaulted to `visibility: members` and `rsvp_enabled: true` (`createPlanEvent` set neither), so every plan — drafts included — appeared on the member calendar as an RSVP-able service, possibly duplicating the church's own Sunday event. Now `visibility: "leaders"`, `rsvp_enabled: false`; the New Plan form explains the staff-only event instead of a bare "optional."
    - **P2 — no orphans.** A failed plan insert left its just-created event behind; the loser of a concurrent first-assignment race left its unlinked event behind too — both members-visible under the old default. Both are now cleaned up. `zonedTimeToInstant` also rolled malformed input over instead of refusing it (`2026-02-30`, `25:99`); it now refuses.
    - **P3 — real shift ids.** `assignVolunteerAction` returned no shift id, so the page made one up (`crypto.randomUUID()`) for a newly assigned volunteer; `removeAssignmentAction` had no row-count check, so Remove on that volunteer deleted 0 rows and reported success — the volunteer vanished from the page, Unfilled rose, and the shift stayed assigned in the database. Remind had the same problem. Both manual assignment and auto-fill now return the real id; removal checks its row count and reports when nothing was removed.
    - **P4 — the plan keeps its event.** After an older plan got its event on first assignment, the page's own state never learned the new event id — saving Plan Details, or clearing the clearable event select, wrote `event_id: null`, and the next assignment created a second event, splitting the plan's shifts across two events. The assignment result now carries the plan's event id to the page and the details form; saving with the field cleared keeps the current event instead of unlinking it.
    - **P5 — tests.** The journey now asserts the shifts' `event_id` equals the plan's and the position's quantity, and that Remove on a just-assigned volunteer really removes the shift. New unit tests for the lost race, a failed plan insert, row-count removal, and malformed `zonedTimeToInstant` input. One `untestedExports` waiver retired (`removeAssignmentAction`).
  - **Not fixed here, tracked in §0.3/§0.5:** the plan's event still doesn't follow a rename or re-date of the plan (moves to §0.5 with G1.8's other deferred half); auto-fill's own assignment notification has no e2e journey coverage (folded into R1's Testing Council run); the build and rotation specs' shared seeded volunteers under `fullyParallel` are a flake-risk watch item, not a row (§0.5).
  - **Correction, our own write-up (seventh consecutive round, see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory):** the New Plan form's "Linked church event (optional)" copy became actively wrong the moment `1233705`'s own fix shipped — leaving it blank no longer means "no event," it means "create a staff-only event" — caught by Agents 2–3 during this same round, not left for a future one. **Also new this round:** an audit agent (Agent 1) briefly broke the read-only rule by creating and deleting a probe file in `lib/`; the working tree was confirmed clean afterwards, and no agent claim was found wrong.

- **Login-id/profile-id mix-up guards — S9; Council Review 26** (`fix/profile-id-guards-s9`, commits `3ab51df` build, `e01f2d4` Council Review 26 fixes, on top of G1.11/`8779048`): closes the row Council Review 21 opened when it found the original mix-up (see `arch_session_profile_ids` memory). A lint rule (`eslint.config.mjs`) bans `session.profile.id` in `app/`, `lib/` and `components/` outside audit-actor use; the six remaining uses of it as an actor id now read `session.userId`, the same login id under a name that says so. `resolveActiveChurchProfileId` and `actions.ts`'s `resolveSessionProfileId` become the one resolver, both returning `session.churchProfileId` (`churchProfileIdFor`, now also excluding merged profiles) instead of each running its own query. The `timeZone` parameters of the church-time, blockout and token-expiry helpers are required, so no caller can silently fall back to UTC. The demo-mode member profile save checks its row count. Council Review 22's `limit 1` self-policy concern doesn't arise: `profiles.user_id` is unique, so a login has exactly one profile — a DB test pins it (`profiles_user_id_uidx`).
  - **Council Review 26** (`docs/reviews/2026-09-30-council-review-26-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped; two combined agents this round — Database & API with Feature, and Route & Page with UX & Shell — both told to write nothing in the repo after Review 25's probe-file breach, and both confirmed clean): S9's changes confirmed correct by both agents. Two clauses found only partial:
    - **"One resolver" was incomplete.** About 9 other per-call profile lookups still ran their own query (`requirePastorProfileContext`, `requireChurchAdminProfileContext`, `actions.ts:2644`, and one each in daily-desk, church-admin, elders, pastor-portal and data-rights actions), several without a `merged_at` filter.
    - **The lint rule's selector was both too narrow and too broad.** It missed destructuring (`const { profile } = session; profile.id`) and bracket access, and separately flagged any `x.profile.id`, not just a session's (a future unrelated `shift.profile.id` join would trip it).
    - **A pre-existing bug found and verified during synthesis, not in S9's diff: `merge_duplicate_profile` was a live privilege escalation, and had never actually worked.** It's `SECURITY DEFINER` and was executable by `anon` and `authenticated`. Its admin check compared `church_memberships.user_id` (a login id) with its `actor_profile_id` argument (a profile id) — the same class of mix-up S9 exists to prevent, but in SQL, invisible to an ESLint rule. Every real admin failed the check; anyone who passed an admin's *login* id as the actor passed it, and could merge (hide) any non-staff profile in that church. Separately, the function had never worked at all: it still read `emergency_contact_*` off `profiles`, which moved to `profile_sensitive_fields` in April (every merge failed with "record has no field"), and a kept profile without an email taking the duplicate's while the duplicate still held it broke `profiles_email_key`. The duplicate's own memberships were also deactivated by profile id, so never.
    - Two pastoral-read audit events (`lib/pastor-portal-data.ts:316, 505`) still recorded a profile id as the actor — the lint can't see a non-session variable.
    - Stale comments: the local dual-path's `merged: false` note, and `lib/church-profile-id.ts:9`.
  - **Council Review 26 fixes (`e01f2d4`), owner-approved P1–P5 (the merge-profile fix, P1, was done ahead of formal approval as a security issue, then approved):**
    - **P1 — `merge_duplicate_profile`.** Migration `20260930000000`: the actor is `auth.uid()`, never the caller's word (`actor_profile_id` is kept for the call signature and must be the caller's own profile); `anon` and `PUBLIC` can no longer execute the function, only `authenticated`; sensitive fields (date of birth, emergency contacts) merge from `profile_sensitive_fields`, filling only what the kept profile lacks; the kept profile's email conflict releases the duplicate's email first; memberships deactivate by login id. **The duplicate's sign-in now moves to the kept profile** — it used to stay on the hidden duplicate, leaving the login with no church profile — **and two profiles that each already have a sign-in are refused, as one person's account, not a duplicate this tool can resolve (owner decision 2026-09-30).** DB tests in `tests/database/profile-merge-auth.test.ts`: an admin can merge; a member can't, even passing an admin's login or profile id; `anon` can't execute it at all.
    - **P2 — audit actors.** The two pastoral reads now use `session.userId`.
    - **P3 — finish "one resolver."** The remaining per-call lookups (pastor, church-admin, elders, daily desk, reviewer context) now read `session.churchProfileId`.
    - **P4 — lint.** The selector is scoped to a session's `profile.id` (no longer flags an unrelated `profile` embed) and adds a second selector for destructuring `profile` from a session.
    - **P5 — small fixes.** Stale comments corrected; the demo profile save returns an error result instead of throwing (a thrown server-action error's message is hidden in a production build).
  - **Not fixed here, tracked separately:** a merge still can't move blocked dates from the source profile to the target (Review 20, already deferred, §0.5); the local dual-path's `loadSupabaseAppDataFromLocalDb` still hard-codes `merged: false` without selecting `merged_at` (low severity — it's the deprecated path under the Supabase-only mandate, `arch_supabase_only` memory — noted, no row).
  - **Correction, our own write-up (eighth consecutive round, see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory):** S9's own commit message (`3ab51df`) overclaimed — it said the audit actors now uniformly read the login id, but two pastoral ones didn't yet, and it called the resolver consolidation "one source of truth" while about 9 lookups still ran their own query. **Also new this round: a combined-agent round (each pairing two of the usual four roles into one report) still found a live privilege escalation entirely outside the diff being reviewed** — a reminder that Council scope-reduction (justified here by the diff's small size, and by Review 25's probe-file incident pushing toward "write nothing") doesn't mean agents stop reading adjacent code the diff touches indirectly (here, the session/profile-id resolvers the diff changed made `merge_duplicate_profile`'s separate SQL-level mix-up newly relevant to check). No agent claim was found wrong.

- **`/hq` role source and tenancy, and the profile self-edit lock — S5; Council Review 27** (`fix/hq-role-tenancy-s5`, commits `43d0305` build, `dcd6d4d` Council Review 27 fixes, on top of S9/`638178e`): a member could set their own `role`, `church_id`, `user_id`, `membership_status`, data-rights approval columns, `is_pastoral`, `safety_clearance_date`, `merged_into_profile_id`/`merged_at` or `member_number` through PostgREST (`profiles_update_self` plus a table-wide UPDATE grant, no column limits). A trigger (`profiles_block_protected_self_edit`) now refuses that on your own profile unless you manage the church there (church admin) or are a platform admin; a change the membership-snapshot sync trigger itself writes is still allowed, so a church admin can demote themselves without hitting the lock. `current_user_role()` — which gates `/hq` and every `hq_*` policy — now reads `church_memberships` instead of the member-settable, secretary-blind `profiles.role`, and `lib/auth.ts` drops its `profiles.role` membership fallback. **Owner decision 2026-09-30, superseding this row's original tenancy framing:** `/hq` is ChurchCore's internal project dashboard, not a church feature — its four tables (`hq_tasks`, `hq_risks`, `hq_decisions`, `hq_sessions`) are now RLS-gated to `is_platform_admin`, not scoped by `church_id` per church as originally planned; every church's admins could previously read and edit it across all churches. Migration `20260930010000`; DB tests in `tests/database/profiles-self-edit-lock.test.ts`.
  - **Council Review 27** (`docs/reviews/2026-09-30-council-review-27-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, two combined agents — Database & API with Feature, Route & Page with UX & Shell, both read-only probes in `BEGIN … ROLLBACK`, tree confirmed clean): the core confirmed correct by both — no member can insert a profile, write a membership or `platform_admins` row, or change a locked column; no member-facing save writes a locked column; staff flows and server-side admin writes are unaffected. Findings, all fixed in `dcd6d4d`:
    - **Critical, fixed ahead of formal approval as a security issue: anyone with the public anon key could erase any member's personal data.** `erase_profile_pii` is `SECURITY DEFINER`, was executable by `anon`, and authorized the actor named in its `actor_profile_id` argument — the identical flaw ADR 0024 named in `merge_duplicate_profile` the round before (Council Review 26), in a second function. A sweep of every `SECURITY DEFINER` function for the same pattern found two more `anon` could call with arguments: `refresh_profile_membership_snapshot` (rewrite any user's profile snapshot) and `generate_member_number`. Fixed in migration `20260930020000`: `erase_profile_pii`'s actor is `auth.uid()`, recorded in the audit; `anon` can no longer execute `erase_profile_pii` or `generate_member_number`; nobody can call `refresh_profile_membership_snapshot` directly (only the owner-run sync trigger does). DB tests in `tests/database/profile-merge-auth.test.ts`: an admin erases; a member naming an admin can't; `anon` has no execute privilege (checked via `has_function_privilege`, not by calling it — see below).
    - **High, fixed: a member could take over another household.** `family_id` wasn't among the locked columns; a member could set it to another family's id, then edit that family and (inferred from the same household check other member actions use) check its people in. Now locked with the rest.
    - **Medium, fixed: `/api/ai` wasn't platform-gated.** The HQ AI advisor checked only that the caller was signed in, so any member of any church could spend Anthropic credit on it, and its `hq_sessions` audit insert then failed silently (RLS). Gated to `is_platform_admin`, like `/hq` itself.
    - **The self-edit exemption was wider than intended.** It used `can_manage_church`, which includes pastor and ministry_leader, so they could still self-set `safety_clearance_date`, the data-rights approval columns and `is_pastoral` — the self-set clearance feeds the clearance report. **Narrowed per owner decision 2026-09-30:** only church admins and platform admins are exempt; everyone else asks an admin, like before S5 for everything else.
    - **A church admin's self-demotion failed with a misleading message.** The membership-snapshot sync re-syncs the profile under the demoted admin's own `auth.uid()`, after `can_manage_church` has already gone false, tripping the lock on a change the *system*, not the member, made. Fixed by exempting changes written from inside that trigger (`pg_trigger_depth() > 1`).
    - **Stale sweep entry.** `KNOWN_BUGS["secretary /hq"]` blamed the old `profiles.role` gate (no longer true) and short-circuited the denied-path assertions; removed, along with `/hq`'s dead `manager`/`teacher` role branches.
    - **Local environment fault, found while testing, not a ChurchCore bug:** in the local Supabase Docker image, calling a `SECURITY DEFINER` function that `anon` isn't granted execute on crashes the local Postgres backend into recovery mode instead of returning "permission denied." The DB tests check execute privilege through `has_function_privilege()` against the catalog instead of calling the function as `anon`; noted in `docs/testing.md`, no tracker row (the hosted service isn't affected — this is local-image-specific).
  - **Correction, our own work (ninth consecutive round, see the `feedback_council_synthesis_scrutiny` memory):** ADR 0024 was written the round before specifically to name this pattern — a `SECURITY DEFINER` function must take its actor from `auth.uid()`, never an argument — but the fix that prompted it checked only `merge_duplicate_profile`, the one function already in hand. `erase_profile_pii` had the identical flaw and sat beside it the whole time; the ADR's own text said "a Council database/API agent checks new or changed `SECURITY DEFINER` functions for this pattern specifically," but nothing prompted a sweep of *existing, unchanged* functions until this round's agents did one anyway. No live agent claim was found wrong this round.

- **Communication-log access alignment — S1; Council Review 28** (`fix/comm-log-access-s1`, commits `3a704f4` build, `4477c5b` Council Review 28 fixes, on top of S5/`dc0566c`): closes the row Council Review 17 opened (F7). `communication_logs`' select and insert RLS policies move from `can_manage_church` to `can_manage_communications` (church admin, pastor, secretary — the same roles the `/app/communications/*` pages admit), so a secretary's history pages stop showing "not found" and a ministry leader, whom the app denies, can no longer read every log through the API. The e2e sweep's `church-admin` identity becomes `nora@graceharbor.church`, a real church admin with no `platform_admins` row, replacing `sarah@churchcoreops.app` — who is also a platform admin, so every prior church-admin check passed through the platform-admin RLS bypass without anyone noticing. The `/hq` manifest entry is corrected to `allowedRoles: []` plus a new `platformAdminOnly: true` field (`scripts/test-surfaces.mjs` now accepts it for the empty-`allowedRoles` check), and the control-plane sweep skip for church-admin is dropped, so nora is now asserted denied there. DB tests in `tests/database/communication-logs-access.test.ts`.
  - **Council Review 28** (`docs/reviews/2026-10-01-council-review-28-synthesis.md`, diff-scoped, one combined agent per the reduced format): confirmed the fix and the nora fixture correct — no reader lost data it should have, and the readiness/operations loaders, the Shepherd AI read, and compose writes (all admin-client or admin-only) are unaffected — then found the same page-gate mismatch one table over, still on `can_manage_church`:
    - **High (verified): `cancelScheduledMessageAction` was a silent no-op.** `communication_logs` has no UPDATE policy, so the action's update through the user's own client matched 0 rows, returned `{ok: true}`, and the cron sent the "cancelled" message anyway. Predates S1.
    - **Medium (verified): `communication_delivery_events` select/insert** — a secretary's message detail and analytics showed zeros; a ministry leader could read delivery events, including recipients' contact details, through the API.
    - **Medium (verified): `communication_suppressions` select/insert** — a ministry leader could read (and add) suppressions on any member's email or phone; a secretary saw none.
    - **Medium (verified data, inferred UX), deferred:** the church-admin dashboard care queue is silently empty for a real church admin, because `can_access_pastoral_data` admits pastors only — reads as "nothing to do," not "restricted." The platform-admin bypass hid this difference until nora's pure church-admin fixture existed. Owner-deferred as a new §0.5 row (UX/copy, outside S1's scope).
    - **Low (verified):** stale sarah comments, a leftover control-plane sweep skip, a DB test checking reads only, and the validator's error text not naming `platformAdminOnly`.
